## Session 505: federal_programs_postgres_migration - Data Architecture

**Date:** 2026-01-10
**Item:** federal_programs_postgres_migration (data_architecture > data_source_unification)
**Priority:** P0

### Summary

Migrated federal programs data from static JSON files to PostgreSQL with proper schema design. Created two tables: `federal_programs` for national program definitions and `federal_program_allocations` for jurisdiction-specific allocations per fiscal year.

### Work Completed

1. **Schema Design:**
   - `federal_programs` table: program_id, program_name, administering_agency, topic, cfda_number, eligible_activities (JSONB), compliance_requirements (JSONB), citizen_participation (JSONB), keywords (JSONB), key_contacts (JSONB), verification_status, temporal versioning
   - `federal_program_allocations` table: program_id, jurisdiction_id, fiscal_year, allocation_amount_cents, allocation_status, local_allocation_model, administering_entity, application_process (JSONB), compliance_requirements (JSONB), key_contacts (JSONB), metadata (JSONB), temporal versioning

2. **CRUD Methods Added to PostgresBackend:**
   - `store_federal_programs()`, `get_federal_programs()`, `get_federal_programs_count()`
   - `store_federal_program_allocations()`, `get_federal_program_allocations()`, `get_federal_program_allocations_count()`

3. **Protocol Updates:**
   - Added method stubs to `StorageBackend` protocol in `backend.py`
   - Added stub implementations to `SQLiteBackend` (returns empty - federal programs stored in PostgreSQL)
   - Updated mock classes in `test_storage_protocols.py`

4. **Ingestion Script:**
   - Created `scripts/ingest_federal_programs.py` to load JSON files into PostgreSQL
   - Loads from `data/funding/federal/*.json` (national definitions)
   - Loads from `data/jurisdiction_overrides/*.json` (jurisdiction allocations)

5. **Data Migrated:**
   - 10 federal programs (CDBG, HOME, Section 8, LIHTC, transportation grants, etc.)
   - 9 allocations across 7 jurisdictions (San Rafael has 3: CDBG, HOME, Section 8)

### Key Files Changed

- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py` - Table schema and CRUD methods (+560 lines)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py` - Stub methods (+65 lines)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py` - Protocol definitions (+120 lines)
- `packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py` - Mock methods (+92 lines)
- `scripts/ingest_federal_programs.py` - Ingestion script (new, 210 lines)
- `pilot.json` - Updated status to ready

### Usage

```bash
# Dry run
python scripts/ingest_federal_programs.py --dry-run

# Full ingestion
python scripts/ingest_federal_programs.py

# Verify
python3 -c "
from dotenv import load_dotenv; load_dotenv()
import os
from civic.storage.postgres_backend import PostgresBackend
backend = PostgresBackend(os.environ['DATABASE_URL'])
print(f'Programs: {backend.get_federal_programs_count()}')
print(f'Allocations (San Rafael): {backend.get_federal_program_allocations_count(\"city-san-rafael\")}')
"
```

## Session 492 (Parallel): llm_as_judge - LLM-as-Judge Relevance Scoring

**Date:** 2026-01-10
**Item:** llm_as_judge (test_infrastructure > test_categorization)
**Priority:** P2
**Track:** INFRA (parallel session, different from P0 DATA track)
**Branch:** feature/infra/eval-framework

### Summary

Implemented LLM-as-judge semantic relevance scoring for the eval framework. This replaces simplistic keyword matching with LLM-based evaluation to more accurately measure precision/recall.

### Implementation

1. **LLMRelevanceJudge class** in `scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py`:
   - Uses LLM to score result relevance (0.0-1.0) instead of keyword matching
   - Default model: `gemini-2.0-flash-exp` ($0.075/1M tokens)
   - File-based caching to minimize costs (~$0.001-0.01 per benchmark run)
   - Integrated with existing model_registry and llm_provider infrastructure

2. **CLI Integration:**
   - `--llm-judge` flag enables LLM-based precision scoring
   - `--llm-judge-model MODEL` to select different models
   - `--clear-cache` to clear cached judgments

3. **Metrics Added:**
   - `llm_precision` - LLM-judged precision score
   - `keyword_precision` - Original keyword-based precision (for comparison)
   - `llm_relevance_scores` - Per-result relevance scores
   - LLM judge stats section in report (cost, tokens, cache hit rate)

### Usage

```bash
# Basic run (keyword-based, unchanged)
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py

# With LLM-as-judge (recommended for accurate evaluation)
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py --llm-judge

# With specific model
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py --llm-judge --llm-judge-model gpt-4o-mini

# Clear cache for fresh evaluations
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py --clear-cache
```

### Files Changed
- `scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py` - Added LLMRelevanceJudge class and CLI flags
- `pilot.json` - Updated llm_as_judge status to ready

### Cost Estimation
- ~100-200 tokens per judgment
- 80 judgments per full benchmark (8 queries × 10 results max)
- gemini-2.0-flash-exp: ~$0.001 per run
- gpt-4o-mini: ~$0.01 per run
- Caching reduces repeated runs to near-zero cost

## Session 490: municipal_code_vectors - Complete Vector Indexing

**Date:** 2026-01-08
**Item:** municipal_code_vectors (pilot_validation > e2e_cloud_data_verification)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Completed municipal code vector indexing for San Rafael. Indexed 5,857 vectors from 3,811 current municipal code sections using 4 parallel Modal workers with COPY bulk inserts.

### Key Findings

1. **Record Count Clarification:**
   - Total DB records: 16,175 (includes historical versions with valid_to != NULL)
   - Current records: 3,811 (valid_to IS NULL - the live sections)
   - Vector count: 5,857 (chunking ratio ~1.54 chunks/doc for large sections)

2. **Parallel Indexing Performance:**
   - 4 workers, COPY bulk inserts (`use_copy=True`)
   - ~4 minutes total (vs ~10 minutes for serial)
   - Each worker processed ~1,465 chunks

3. **Search Quality Verified:**
   - `PgVectorBackend.search(corpus_type='municipal_code')` returns relevant results
   - Scores 0.70-0.76 for topical queries
   - Example: "accessory dwelling unit zoning" → Section 14.16.285 (ADU regulations)

### Integration Gap Discovered

**IMPORTANT:** `what_applies()` does NOT use the municipal_code vectors yet.

- `context.py` uses `CivicEmbeddings.has_municipal_code()` → returns `False`
- `CivicEmbeddings` is ChromaDB-based, not PgVector
- Municipal code vectors are in PgVector, unreachable from current API

**Solution:** Tracked by existing items:
- `vector_backend_unification` (P1) - Make CivicEmbeddings delegate to VectorBackend
- `pgvector_cross_corpus_search` (P1) - Unified query interface

### Files Changed

- `pilot.json` - Updated municipal_code_vectors status to `ready`, documented integration gap

### Commands Used

```bash
# Index with parallel workers
modal run scripts/modal_vectors.py --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --corpus municipal_code --reindex --parallel 4

# Verify search
python3 -c "
from civic.storage.pgvector_backend import PgVectorBackend
import os
pgvector = PgVectorBackend(os.getenv('DATABASE_URL'), provider_type='fastembed')
results = pgvector.search('ADU zoning', 'city-san-rafael', 'municipal_code', top_k=3)
"
```

### Next P0

`pgvector_cross_corpus_search` - Unified query interface for cross-corpus vector search on pgvector. Enables what_applies() to use the municipal_code vectors.

## Session 434: budget_schema - Add Budget Items Table Schema

**Date:** 2026-01-02
**Item:** budget_schema (data_readiness > budget)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Added budget_items schema to StorageBackend protocol and PostgresBackend implementation. This enables budget queries like "How much does San Rafael spend on Police?" by providing a structured way to store and query municipal/county budget line items.

### Implementation

1. **StorageBackend protocol** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py`):
   - `store_budget_items(jurisdiction_id, items, as_of, use_copy) -> int`
   - `get_budget_items(jurisdiction_id, fiscal_year, fund, department, as_of, limit) -> List[Dict]`
   - `get_budget_summary(jurisdiction_id, fiscal_year, group_by, as_of) -> List[Dict]`
   - `get_budget_items_count(jurisdiction_id, fiscal_year) -> int`

2. **PostgresBackend** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py`):
   - Created `budget_items` table with temporal versioning
   - Amounts stored in BIGINT cents (not decimals) for precision
   - Indexes on (jurisdiction_id, fiscal_year), (department), (fund)
   - COPY strategy for bulk inserts (10x faster than execute_values)
   - CHECK constraints ensure non-negative amounts

3. **SQLiteBackend stubs** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py`):
   - Added stub implementations returning empty/0 (Postgres used in production)

4. **Test mocks** (`packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py`):
   - Added budget methods to MockStorageBackend and InMemoryStorage classes

### Schema Design

```sql
CREATE TABLE budget_items (
    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    item_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    jurisdiction_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    fiscal_year TEXT NOT NULL,
    fund TEXT,
    department TEXT,
    program TEXT,
    line_item TEXT NOT NULL,
    budgeted_cents BIGINT NOT NULL,
    revised_cents BIGINT,
    actual_cents BIGINT,
    source_url TEXT,
    source_page INTEGER,
    notes TEXT,
    metadata JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
    valid_from TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
    valid_to TIMESTAMP,
    UNIQUE (item_id, jurisdiction_id, valid_from),
    CHECK (budgeted_cents >= 0),
    CHECK (revised_cents IS NULL OR revised_cents >= 0),
    CHECK (actual_cents IS NULL OR actual_cents >= 0)
);
```

### Test Results

```
pytest tests/test_storage_protocols.py → 69 passed
pytest tests/test_civic.py → 34 passed (smoke tests)
```

### Files Changed
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py (+75 lines)
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py (+350 lines)
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py (+45 lines)
- packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py (+72 lines)
- pilot.json (status: ready, priority: 2)

## Session 378: r2_source_caching - Cache Scraped Content in R2

**Date:** 2025-12-27
**Item:** r2_source_caching (pipeline_automation > cloud_integration)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Added SourceCache class for caching raw scraped content (HTML pages, PDFs) in blob storage (R2/local). This reduces repeat HTTP requests during development and pipeline reruns.

### Implementation

1. **SourceCache class** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cache.py`):
   - `cache_key(url)`: Generates deterministic SHA256 hash key for URL
   - `get(url)`: Returns cached content if not expired, None otherwise
   - `put(url, content, ttl_hours)`: Stores content with TTL metadata
   - `invalidate(url)`: Removes specific cached entry
   - `clear()`: Removes all cached entries
   - `stats()`: Returns hits, misses, hit_rate, entry_count
   - Works with both LocalBlobBackend and R2Backend
   - Cache key format: `source-cache/{url_hash[:16]}`

2. **CachedSession class** (`cache.py`):
   - Drop-in replacement for requests.Session with automatic caching
   - Checks cache before HTTP requests
   - Caches successful responses automatically
   - Supports force_refresh to bypass cache

3. **ProudCityClient integration** (`clients/proudcity.py`):
   - Added optional `cache: SourceCache` parameter to `__init__`
   - `_make_request()` checks cache before HTTP requests
   - Caches successful responses with configurable TTL
   - Logs cache hits/misses for debugging

4. **ProudCitySource integration** (`clients/proudcity.py`):
   - Added `cache` param to constructor and factory methods
   - `cache_stats()` method returns cache statistics
   - Updated docs with cache usage example

5. **CLI integration** (`cli/discover.py`):
   - Added `--cache` flag to enable caching
   - Added `--cache-ttl` option (default 24 hours)
   - Added `--clear-cache` flag to clear before run
   - Logs cache stats after pipeline completion

6. **Tests** (`test_source_cache.py`, 20 tests):
   - SourceCache: key generation, get/put, expiration, invalidate, clear, stats
   - CachedSession: cache hit/miss, force refresh
   - R2/Local backend metadata handling
   - ProudCityClient/Source cache integration

### Usage

```bash
# Enable caching for discovery pipeline
civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --cache

# With custom TTL
civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --cache --cache-ttl 48

# Clear cache before running
civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --cache --clear-cache
```

### Results

- 20 cache tests: ✅ passed
- 34 smoke tests: ✅ passed
- 52 client tests: ✅ passed

### Next P0: youtube_cloud_storage
Wire youtube.py CLI to store video metadata in Postgres instead of local JSON.

## Session 349: validate_meetings_on_ingest - JSON Schema Validation for Pipeline

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** validate_meetings_on_ingest (data_standards > schema_validation)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Added JSON schema validation to the meeting ingestion pipeline. Meetings are now validated before storage, with invalid meetings filtered and logged.

### Implementation

1. **Meeting Schema Module** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/meeting_schema.py`):
   - `MEETING_SCHEMA`: JSON Schema for Meeting objects (required fields: id, title, meeting_datetime, jurisdiction_id)
   - `MeetingValidator`: Validates single meetings or batches using jsonschema library
   - `MeetingValidationResult`: Per-meeting validation result (is_valid, errors, warnings)
   - `BatchValidationResult`: Batch summary (total/valid/invalid counts, timing, valid_meetings list)

2. **Pipeline Integration** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/pipeline.py`):
   - Added `validate_on_ingest: bool = True` parameter to Pipeline.__init__
   - Modified `_run_store()` to validate meetings before storage
   - Validation metadata stored in stage status: total/valid/invalid counts, timing
   - Invalid meetings logged as warnings with details
   - Filter by default (invalid meetings excluded from storage)

3. **Exports** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/__init__.py`):
   - Exported MeetingValidator, MeetingValidationResult, BatchValidationResult, MEETING_SCHEMA

4. **Tests** (26 total):
   - `test_meeting_schema.py`: 21 tests for schema, validator, batch validation, strict mode, datetime handling
   - `test_pipeline_checkpoint.py`: 5 new tests for pipeline validation integration

5. **Test Fix**:
   - Updated `test_clients.py` to expect 4 stages (discover, ingest, store, index) instead of 3

### Key Features
- Non-blocking by default: Invalid meetings are logged and filtered, pipeline continues
- Strict mode available: `MeetingValidator(strict=True)` raises on invalid
- Graceful degradation: Works without jsonschema installed (warning only)
- Performance tracking: Validation time recorded in stage metadata
- Disable option: `Pipeline(..., validate_on_ingest=False)` skips validation

- 5 pipeline validation tests: passed

## Session 339: storage_stats_abstraction - Fix Layer Boundary Violation

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** storage_stats_abstraction (city_onboarding > orchestration)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Fixed layer boundary violation where the API server (`civic-services`) imported `SQLiteBackend` directly from `civic.storage`. This broke the abstraction needed for backend swapping (SQLite → PostgreSQL).

### Implementation

1. **Core API Enhancement** (`packages/civic/src/civic/civic.py`):
   - Added `_storage: StorageBackend` field to Civic dataclass
   - Added `get_storage_stats(jurisdiction_id: str = None) -> StorageStats` method
   - Initializes `SQLiteBackend` in `__post_init__`

2. **StorageStats Enhancement** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py`):
   - Added `size_bytes` calculation (file size for SQLite)
   - Added `backend_type` to `metadata` dict
   - Error-safe file size check with OSError handling

3. **Public API Export** (`packages/civic/src/civic/__init__.py`):
   - Exported `StorageStats` for type hints

4. **API Server Fix** (`civic_api_integrated.py:7456-7482`):
   - Replaced `from civic.storage.sqlite_backend import SQLiteBackend` with `from civic import Civic`
   - Now uses `Civic(jurisdiction_id).get_storage_stats()`
   - Backend type comes from `storage_stats.metadata.get('backend_type', 'unknown')`

### Testing
- 34 smoke tests: passed
- 31 storage-related tests: passed
- Manual verification: `Civic.get_storage_stats()` returns correct data

### Why This Matters
1. **Open-source adoption**: Other municipalities copying patterns won't hard-code SQLite
2. **PostgreSQL migration**: Clean abstraction enables backend swap
3. **Layer discipline**: Coordination layer (civic-services) uses only Core layer public API (civic)

### Pilot Progress
- Before: 141/168 items (83.9%)
- After: 142/168 items (84.5%)
- 1 item completed: storage_stats_abstraction

### Next P0
`postgres_backend_implementation` - Create PostgresBackend implementing StorageBackend protocol to prove modularity

5. **Human Summary** - `summary()` method for terminal output

## Session 332: platform_specific_notes - Platform Technical Reference

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** platform_specific_notes (city_onboarding > documentation)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created comprehensive technical reference documenting quirks and patterns for all three civic meeting platforms: Legistar, CivicClerk, and ProudCity.

### Implementation

**New File:** `docs/user_guides/PLATFORM_SPECIFIC_NOTES.md`

Covers:
- **Quick Reference Table** - Platform detection, API types, example cities
- **Legistar (REST API)** - Base URL patterns, date formats (ISO 8601 with datetime wrapper), rate limiting (1.0s throttle), retryable errors (429/500/502/503), meeting type inference
- **CivicClerk (OData v4)** - Subdomain patterns, OData filter syntax with millisecond timestamps, pagination ($top/$skip), event enrichment workflow
- **ProudCity (Web Scraping)** - Archive discovery, slug-to-key conversion, date extraction from URLs, PDF extraction patterns, coverage analysis
- **Cross-Platform Patterns** - DataSource protocol, error handling, Meeting normalization
- **Adding New Platforms** - Implementation guide for new civic platforms

### Key Details Documented
- Legistar: `EventDate` and `EventTime` are separate fields requiring combination
- CivicClerk: URL encoding required for filter values; enrichment needed for agenda URLs
- ProudCity: Rate limited to 1 req/sec; uses regex pattern matching for date extraction

Covers 10 major categories:
1. **Startup Issues** - Application won't start, missing secrets, health check timeouts
2. **Authentication Issues** - 401 errors, invalid API keys, CORS problems

6. **Data Extraction Issues** - No meetings found, Legistar/SeeClickFix/YouTube errors
7. **RAG/Search Issues** - Vector search returns nothing, irrelevant results
8. **Resource Issues** - OOM, disk full, large Docker images
9. **Deployment Issues** - Deploy fails, version mismatch between apps
10. **Recovery Procedures** - Quick rollback, full rollback, emergency procedures

Includes:

### Summary

### Implementation

**New CLI:** `civic-extract discover`

Features:
- One-time run: `civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael`
- Scheduled mode: `--schedule` runs daily at 6am using `schedule` library
- Dry-run mode: `--dry-run` validates config without running pipeline
- Checkpoint support: Saves/resumes from `data/checkpoints/{jurisdiction}.json`
- Configurable: `--days-past`, `--days-ahead`, `--checkpoint-dir`

### Usage
```bash
# One-time run
civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael

# Start scheduler (daily at 6am)
civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --schedule

# Dry-run validation
civic-extract discover --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --dry-run
```

- Discovered 850 total meetings across 15 archive types
- Ingested 17 meetings within date range
- Checkpoint saved to data/checkpoints/city-san-rafael.json
- Pipeline completed in ~46 seconds

### Architecture Decision
Implemented as package CLI (`civic-extract`) rather than `scripts/` because:

- Sets pattern for future commands (youtube, transcribe, etc.)

## Session 318: checkpoint_for_meetings - Pipeline checkpoint/resume capability

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** checkpoint_for_meetings (city_onboarding > orchestration)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Extended checkpoint/resume capability to the Pipeline class for meeting discovery/ingestion. Previously only transcription batch processing had resume capability. Now the generalized Pipeline supports resuming from a checkpoint after failures.

### Implementation

**New dataclass:** `IngestCheckpoint` in `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/pipeline.py`
- Tracks last successfully ingested meeting (id + datetime)
- Serializable to/from JSON for persistence

**Pipeline.run() parameters:**
```python
from civic_extraction import Pipeline, IngestCheckpoint, save_checkpoint, load_checkpoint

# Resume from checkpoint
checkpoint = load_checkpoint("data/checkpoints/city-san-rafael.json")
result = pipeline.run(
    resume_from=checkpoint,  # Skip meetings at or before checkpoint
    on_checkpoint=lambda cp: save_checkpoint(cp, path),  # Called after ingest
)
```

**Checkpoint utilities:**
- `save_checkpoint(checkpoint, path)` - Save to JSON file
- `load_checkpoint(path)` - Load from JSON (returns None if not found)
- `checkpoint_path_for_jurisdiction(jurisdiction_id)` - Standard path generator

**Filtering logic:**
- Meetings with datetime > checkpoint.last_meeting_datetime are processed
- Meetings with same datetime but different ID are processed
- Exact match (same datetime + ID) is skipped

### Files Modified
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/pipeline.py` - IngestCheckpoint, utilities, Pipeline.run()
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/__init__.py` - Export new symbols
- `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_pipeline_checkpoint.py` - 14 tests

### Tests
- 14 checkpoint tests passing
- 34 smoke tests passing

### Next Steps
Related items enabled by this:
- `last_processed_tracking` (P2) - Track last processed timestamp per pipeline
- `skip_already_processed` (P2) - Skip items already in database
- `failure_handling` (P2) - Retry failed items

## Session 317: platform_detection_helper - Auto-detect city platform

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** platform_detection_helper (city_onboarding > configuration)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented platform detection helper for city onboarding. The `detect_platform()` function probes Legistar API, CivicClerk OData API, and scrapes ProudCity /meetings/ pages to auto-detect which platform a city uses.

### Implementation

**New module:** `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/platform_detection.py`

**Main function:**
```python
from civic_extraction import detect_platform

result = detect_platform("https://www.cityofsanrafael.org")
# DetectionResult(source_type='proudcity', confidence=0.90, ...)
```

**Detection strategies:**
1. **Legistar** (0.95 confidence): Test `webapi.legistar.com/v1/{client}/bodies`
2. **CivicClerk** (0.95 confidence): Test `{subdomain}.api.civicclerk.com/v1/Boards`
3. **ProudCity** (0.50-0.90 confidence): Scrape /meetings/ for archive links

**Confidence scoring:**
- 0.95: Valid API JSON response
- 0.90: 5+ meeting type archives found
- 0.75: 2-4 archives found
- 0.50: 1 archive found
- 0.0: No platform detected

### Files Created
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/platform_detection.py` - Detection module
- `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_platform_detection.py` - 27 unit tests

### Files Modified
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/__init__.py` - Export detect_platform, DetectionResult
- `pilot.json` - Updated platform_detection_helper to "ready"

### Tests
- 27 platform detection tests passing
- 34 smoke tests passing

### Next Steps
This enables future city onboarding flow:
1. User provides city URL
2. `detect_platform()` returns source_type + confidence
3. If confidence >= 0.8, auto-generate extraction config
4. If low confidence, prompt for manual platform selection

## Session 315: preflight_checks + Storage Architecture Discovery

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** preflight_checks (city_onboarding > validation)
**Priority:** P1

### Summary
Implemented `source.validate()` method on all DataSource clients. Then discovered critical gap: Pipeline fetches meetings but has no persistence layer.

### Critical Discovery: Pipeline Has No Storage

Tested `civic-bootstrap san-rafael --skip-index`:
- discover: 850 items (health check)
- ingest: 17 meetings fetched into memory
- **Problem:** Meetings are discarded when process exits - no disk write!

### Architecture Decision for Next Session

**4-stage pipeline:** discover → ingest → store → index

**Two protocols in `civic/storage/`:**
```python
class StorageBackend(Protocol):  # Primary storage (source of truth)
    def save_meetings(self, meetings: List[Meeting]) -> int
    def get_meetings(self, jurisdiction_id: str) -> List[Meeting]

class VectorBackend(Protocol):  # Search index (derived)
    def index(self, meetings: List[Meeting]) -> int
    def search(self, query: str, k: int) -> List[SearchResult]
    def rebuild(self, storage: StorageBackend) -> int
```

**Key principle:** Index reads from StorageBackend, not memory. Vector index is always derived from primary storage.

**Implementations:**
- StorageBackend: SQLiteStorage (pilot), PostgresStorage (scale)
- VectorBackend: ChromaDBBackend (pilot), PgVectorBackend (scale)

**1. ValidationResult Dataclass** (base.py)
- `is_valid` - all checks passed, safe to proceed
- `config_valid` - config structure is correct
- `api_reachable` - API endpoint is accessible
- `errors[]` - critical errors (fail fast)
- `warnings[]` - non-blocking issues
- `check_duration_ms` - timing info
- `metadata` - platform-specific context

**2. DataSource Protocol** (base.py)
- Added `validate() -> ValidationResult` method signature
- Preflight check that fails fast before pipeline runs

**3. ProudCitySource.validate()** (proudcity.py)
- Checks: base_url (required, HTTPS), jurisdiction_id, source_id
- Checks: archives non-empty OR auto_discover enabled
- API check: hits `/meetings/` endpoint
- Archive validation: verifies each archive path is reachable (warnings)

**4. LegistarClient.validate()** (legistar.py)
- Checks: client_name required
- API check: hits `/bodies` endpoint

**5. CivicClerkClient.validate()** (civicclerk.py)
- Checks: subdomain required
- API check: hits `/Boards` endpoint

### Tests Added
- TestValidationResult: 3 tests (creation, with errors, to_dict)
- TestValidateMethods: 6 tests (all client types, config validation)
- Total: 52 extraction client tests passing

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/base.py` - ValidationResult, DataSource.validate()
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/proudcity.py` - ProudCitySource.validate()
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/legistar.py` - LegistarClient.validate()
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/civicclerk.py` - CivicClerkClient.validate()
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/__init__.py` - Export ValidationResult
- `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_clients.py` - 9 new tests
- `pilot.json` - Updated preflight_checks to "ready"

### Tests
- 52 extraction client tests passing
- 34 smoke tests passing

**Commit:** Session 315: preflight_checks - DataSource validate() method

## Session 304: auto_discover_meeting_types - Dynamic meeting type discovery
**Date:** 2025-12-18
**Item:** auto_discover_meeting_types (ingestion_visibility > source_discovery)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented `discover_meeting_types()` method in ProudCityClient that programmatically finds all meeting type archives by scraping the main /meetings/ page. This replaces the need for hardcoded meeting types.

### Implementation
Added `discover_meeting_types()` method to `proudcity.py` that:
1. Fetches `{base_url}/meetings/` landing page
2. Finds all links matching `/-meetings/` or `/-hearings/` pattern
3. Extracts type key from URL path (e.g., `/city-council-meetings/` → `city_council`)
4. Returns dict mapping type keys to archive paths

### Test Results
- Live test on San Rafael: Discovers **15 meeting types** vs 6 hardcoded in DEFAULT_ARCHIVES
- New types discovered: ada_access_advisory_committee, bicycle_pedestrian_advisory_committee, board_and_commission, board_of_library_trustees, guide_public, paac, park_and_recreation_commission, pickleweed_advisory_committee, public_art_review_board, and more

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/proudcity.py` - Added discover_meeting_types() method
- `pilot.json` - Updated auto_discover_meeting_types to "ready"

### Tests
- 34 smoke tests passing
- 23 extraction client tests passing

**Commit:** Session 304: auto_discover_meeting_types - Dynamic meeting type discovery

## Session 293: log_levels_appropriate - Standardize log levels
**Date:** 2025-12-17
**Item:** log_levels_appropriate (monitoring_observability > logging)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Standardized log levels across the codebase to follow best practices:
- **DEBUG**: Workflow steps, fallback conditions, routine operations
- **INFO**: Significant events (workflow start, batch imports)
- **WARNING**: Recoverable errors (failed cache save, cleanup failure)
- **ERROR**: Actual errors affecting functionality

### Changes Made
1. **content_cache.py** - Removed emojis, changed routine logs to DEBUG (10 changes)
2. **issue_matcher.py** - Changed fallback conditions to DEBUG (2 changes)
3. **conversation_service.py** - Changed missing directory to DEBUG (1 change)
4. **coordination/nodes.py** - Changed workflow steps to DEBUG (4 changes)
5. **coordination/preparation_nodes.py** - Changed workflow steps to DEBUG (6 changes)
6. **coordination/pattern_nodes.py** - Changed workflow steps to DEBUG (10 changes)
7. **coordination/strategy_nodes.py** - Changed workflow steps to DEBUG (6 changes)
8. **coordination/suggestion_nodes.py** - Changed workflow steps to DEBUG (5 changes)
9. **meetings/reconciliation.py** - Changed fallback conditions to DEBUG (2 changes)

## Session 254: backup_before_deploy - Pre-deployment backup procedure
Date: 2025-12-11

### Work Done
1. **Created PRE_DEPLOYMENT_BACKUP.md** (`docs/critical/PRE_DEPLOYMENT_BACKUP.md`):
   - Quick reference section with essential commands
   - 6-step detailed procedure:
     1. Pre-backup checks (verify environment, disk space, DB health)
     2. Create backup using scripts/backup.py
     3. Verify backup created and listed
     4. Verify backup integrity (checksum, SQLite integrity)
     5. Optional local copy download via SFTP
     6. Record deployment context (timestamp, version, reason)
   - Troubleshooting section (permission denied, disk full, integrity failures, SSH issues)
   - Verification checklist for pre-deployment sign-off
   - Rollback reference with quick restore commands
   - Links to related docs (DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE.md, ROLLBACK_PROCEDURES.md)

   - Marked `backup_before_deploy` as ready
   - Added completion note with artifact details

## Session 243: backup_script - Database backup procedure
Date: 2025-12-11

### Work Done
1. **Created backup script** (`scripts/backup.py`):
   - Timestamped backup files with SHA256 checksums
   - SQLite backup API for consistent snapshots
   - Optional gzip compression (1.9MB -> 354KB)
   - SQLite integrity verification before restore
   - Retention policy: 7 daily + 4 weekly backups
   - Safety: requires --force to overwrite existing DB

2. **Commands available**:
   ```bash
   python scripts/backup.py                     # Backup all databases
   python scripts/backup.py --compress          # Backup with gzip
   python scripts/backup.py --restore BACKUP    # Restore from backup
   python scripts/backup.py --status            # Show backup status
   python scripts/backup.py --list              # List available backups
   python scripts/backup.py --verify BACKUP     # Verify backup integrity
   python scripts/backup.py --clean             # Remove old backups
   python scripts/backup.py --dry-run           # Preview actions
   ```

3. **Backup format**:
   - Filename: `{db_name}_{YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS}.db[.gz]`
   - Checksum file: `.sha256` companion file
   - Location: `data/backups/`

### Progress
- Pilot items: 19/116 ready (16%)
- backup_script: not_ready -> ready
- Next P1 items: hosting_decision, cost_estimate, deployment_documented

Session 218: unified_vector_store - Refactor VectorStore to use EmbeddingProvider

### Summary
Refactored VectorStore to accept any EmbeddingProvider via dependency injection,
eliminating hardcoded OpenAI dependency and enabling provider flexibility.

### Changes
1. **Refactored packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/legal/embeddings/store.py**
   - Replaced openai_api_key parameter with provider: EmbeddingProvider
   - Default provider is local SentenceTransformer (no API key required)
   - Collection metadata now tracks embedding_model and embedding_dimension
   - Added _validate_dimension() to prevent mismatched provider usage
   - Fixed ChromaDB empty metadata issue with placeholder
   - Removed OpenAI import dependency

2. **Updated packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/legal/embeddings/indexer.py**
   - LegalIndexer accepts provider parameter, passes to VectorStore
   - Backward compatible: None defaults to local provider

3. **Updated packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/legal/retrieval/search.py**
   - LegalSearch accepts provider parameter, passes to VectorStore
   - Backward compatible: None defaults to local provider

4. **Created packages/civic/tests/test_unified_vector_store.py** (18 tests)
   - TestVectorStoreWithLocalProvider: 6 tests (create, add/search, filter, delete, metadata)
   - TestVectorStoreWithOpenAIProvider: 2 tests (create, add/search with mocked API)
   - TestDimensionValidation: 3 tests (mismatch detection, same dimension, empty collection)
   - TestVectorStoreIntegration: 4 tests (batch, persistence, semantic quality, scores)
   - TestLegalIndexerAndSearchIntegration: 3 tests (provider injection, compatibility)

### Key Design Decisions
- **Default to local**: VectorStore() without arguments uses SentenceTransformer
- **Dimension validation**: Opening existing index with wrong-dimension provider raises error
- **Metadata tracking**: Collection stores embedding_model and embedding_dimension for validation
- **Backward compatible**: LegalIndexer/LegalSearch accept provider but default to local

### Results
- 18 new tests passing
- 83 targeted tests passing (smoke + embedding provider + unified store)
- No OpenAI API key required for default usage
- Ready for production use with either provider

- unified_vector_store: not_tested → tested

### Tests

2. test_testimony_content_captured_in_transcript: Validates transcript contains first-person testimony, shelter discussion, multiple speakers.

6. test_timestamp_precision: Transcript has millisecond timestamps; minutes have meeting start/end only.
7. test_information_loss_quantification: Minutes capture <5% of Item 6.a content (transcript 100x+ more chars).
8. test_speaker_affiliation_loss: 9 org speakers listed in minutes but no testimony content captured.
9. test_video_linking_capability: Transcript timestamps enable YouTube URL construction for direct linking.

- Item 6.a duration: 261.6 minutes (4.4 hours)
- Public testimony: 168 chunks, 147 minutes - minutes only list names
- Staff Q&A: 19 chunks, 39 minutes - completely missing from minutes
- Deliberation: 17 chunks, 22 minutes - completely missing from minutes
- Content ratio: Transcript ~100x more content than minutes for same item

## Session 237: Decision-Transcript Reconciliation System

### Summary
Designed and implemented a consensus-based system to link decisions (from minutes) 
to transcript chunks (from video recordings) using both structural and semantic signals.

### Changes Made

1. **Fixed transcript.py** - Exposed `detect_agenda_items` parameter in `chunk_file()` method
   - Parameter was available in `chunk()` but not passed through from `chunk_file()`
   - Now agenda item detection runs by default when chunking transcripts

2. **Enhanced embeddings.py**:
   - Added `load_video_meeting_map()` utility to load video_id → meeting_date mapping
   - Updated `build_transcripts_index()` with `detect_agenda_items` and `video_meeting_map` parameters
   - Updated `_transcript_chunk_to_metadata()` to include `meeting_date` in chunk metadata

3. **Created reconciliation.py** - New module for decision-transcript linking:
   - `DecisionTranscriptReconciler` class with consensus approach
   - Combines structural matching (agenda_item) + semantic matching (embeddings)
   - When signals agree → high confidence; when disagree → flag anomaly
   - `EdgeCaseHandler` for consent calendar, out-of-order items, revisits, multi-item discussions
   - `ReconciliationResult` and `BatchReconciliationResult` for metrics

### Test Results
- Agenda detection: 275/285 chunks tagged with agenda_item for Nov 17 meeting
- Reconciliation: 37.5% decision coverage, 69.8% transcript coverage
- 6 agenda items detected: 4.a, 5.a, 6, 6.a, consent, public_hearing
- All 31 smoke tests pass

### Architecture Decision
Consensus approach vs fallback: Both structural and semantic signals contribute to 
confidence scoring rather than semantic being a fallback. Agreement bonus when signals 
align, penalty when they disagree. This enables both query enrichment and data validation.

### Next Steps
- Update query layer with `what_happened_full_context()` method
- Write batch script to re-index all transcripts with new agenda detection
- Test semantic matching with embeddings client

   - Shared volume mount for SQLite + ChromaDB
   - Health checks configured
   - Auto-start/stop for cost efficiency

3. **Cost breakdown** (~$4.73/month total):
   - civic-api: $1.94/month (shared-cpu-1x, 256MB)
   - civic-websocket: $1.94/month (shared-cpu-1x, 256MB)
   - Persistent volume (3GB): $0.45/month
   - Data egress (~2GB): $0.40/month
   - Buffer: ~$2.27/month under $7 limit

### Technical Decisions
- **Platform**: Fly.io (vs Railway, Render, DigitalOcean)
- **Region**: SJC (closest to San Rafael)
- **Instance type**: shared-cpu-1x, 256MB (sufficient for pilot)
- **Storage**: 3GB persistent volume for databases

   - `HumanReadableFormatter`: Colored console output for development
   - `with_correlation_id()`: Context manager for request tracing across async operations
   - `configure_logging()`: Sets up file (JSON + text) and console handlers
   - Helper functions: `log_request_start()`, `log_request_complete()`, `log_error()`, `log_audit()`

2. **Updated `src/civic_api_integrated.py`**:
   - Replaced module initialization `print()` statements with structured `logger.debug/warning` calls
   - Added request logging with correlation IDs in `do_GET()` and `do_POST()`
   - Updated `log_message()` to use structured format
   - Simplified server startup logging

3. **Updated `src/civic_socketio_server.py`**:
   - Added structured logging import with fallback
   - Updated all event handlers (connect, disconnect, join_thread, new_message) to use structured format
   - Added extra fields: sid, user_id, thread_id, reason for failures

4. **Log output files**:
   - `logs/civic.json.log`: JSON-formatted logs for machine parsing/aggregation
   - `logs/civic.log`: Human-readable text logs

### JSON Log Format
```json
{
  "timestamp": "2025-12-11T20:24:09.458522+00:00",
  "level": "ERROR",
  "logger": "test_module",
  "message": "test_error",
  "correlation_id": "eeba363f",
  "extra": {"error_type": "ValueError"},
  "exception": {
    "type": "ValueError",
    "message": "Test error",
    "traceback": "..."
  }
}
```

### Progress
- Pilot items: 28/116 ready (24%)
- structured_logging: not_ready -> ready
- request_logging: not_ready -> ready
- error_context: not_ready -> ready
- Next P1 items: log_levels_appropriate, uptime_monitoring

## Session 252: rollback_documented - Create rollback procedures guide
Date: 2025-12-11

### Work Done
1. **Created comprehensive rollback guide** (`docs/critical/ROLLBACK_PROCEDURES.md`):
   - Quick reference table for emergency rollbacks
   - Decision framework: when to roll back vs investigate vs fix forward
   - Code-only rollback using `fly releases` and `fly deploy --image`
   - Full rollback with code + data restore workflow
   - Coordinated two-app rollback (civic-api + civic-websocket must stay in sync)
   - Data-only restore using existing `scripts/backup.py`
   - Post-rollback verification checklist
   - Common rollback scenarios with step-by-step solutions
   - Prevention checklist for pre-deployment safety

2. **Updated DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE.md**:
   - Added link to ROLLBACK_PROCEDURES.md in troubleshooting section
   - Added rollback guide to Related Documentation table

### Key Content in Rollback Guide
- **Quick Rollback (5-10 min)**: For code bugs when data is intact
- **Full Rollback (15-20 min)**: For code + data issues
- **Coordinated Two-App Rollback**: Ensures civic-api and civic-websocket stay compatible
- **Data-Only Restore**: When code is fine but data was corrupted
- **Scenarios covered**: Broken migrations, memory issues, version mismatch, data corruption

### Verification
- Document follows existing DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE.md patterns (tables, code blocks, sections)
- Cross-referenced with backup.py capabilities
- Comprehensive enough for operator to recover from common failure modes

## Session 256: backup_schedule - Daily backup schedule
Date: 2025-12-11

### Work Done
1. **Created GitHub Actions workflow** (`.github/workflows/daily-backup.yml`):
   - Scheduled daily at 2:00 AM UTC (6:00 PM Pacific)
   - Manual trigger support via workflow_dispatch
   - Creates compressed backups using existing scripts/backup.py
   - Verifies backup integrity after creation
   - Applies retention policy (7 daily + 4 weekly)
   - Failure notifications via GitHub issue creation
   - Uses FLY_API_TOKEN secret for Fly.io SSH access

## Session 273: Embedding model migration to nomic-embed-text-v1.5
Date: 2025-12-15

### Work Done
1. **Migrated default embedding model from all-MiniLM-L6-v2 to nomic-ai/nomic-embed-text-v1.5**:
   - Updated `DEFAULT_MODEL` in `embeddings.py` and `provider.py`
   - Changed embedding dimensions from 384 to 768
   - Added `trust_remote_code=True` parameter for models with custom code

2. **Updated FastEmbedProvider for nomic model**:
   - Changed FastEmbedProvider.DEFAULT_MODEL to `nomic-ai/nomic-embed-text-v1.5`
   - Added nomic model to MODEL_DIMENSIONS dict (768 dims)
   - Exported FastEmbedProvider from embeddings __init__.py

3. **Updated all test files**:
   - `test_embedding_provider.py`: Updated dimension assertions (384→768), model name assertions
   - `test_integration_rag_san_rafael.py`: Updated all embedding model/dimension assertions
   - `test_unified_vector_store.py`: Updated dimension assertions
   - `conftest.py`: Updated cached model fixture
   - `test_integration_san_rafael.py`: Updated embedder fixture

4. **Updated documentation**:
   - `VECTOR_RAG_SCHEMA.md`: Updated all embedding model references and dimensions

5. **Updated scripts**:
   - `scripts/build_decision_embeddings.py`: Updated default model and added trust_remote_code

### Model Benefits
- **8192 token context** (vs 128 tokens for MiniLM) - supports longer documents
- **768 dimensions** (vs 384) - better semantic precision
- **Same local inference** - no API costs

### Files Changed

## Session 277: Document SQLite-ChromaDB join patterns
Date: 2025-12-15

### Work Done
Created `docs/critical/SQLITE_CHROMADB_JOIN_PATTERNS.md` documenting the ID patterns and join strategies enabling correlation between SQLite and ChromaDB data stores.

### Key Findings
1. **Decision IDs**: Actual format `20251117-item-6a` differs from documented `city-san-rafael-2025-11-17-6a`
   - Join via metadata: `meeting_date` + `agenda_item`

2. **Chunk IDs**: Positional (`chunk-42`), not globally unique
   - Join via metadata: `agenda_item` field

3. **Transcript IDs**: Format `transcript-{video_id}-{chunk_index}`
   - Join via `video_url` LIKE or metadata

4. **Issue IDs**: Format `{jurisdiction}-scf-{source_id}` 
   - **Direct ID join** with SQLite `issues.id` (most reliable pattern)

5. **Municipal Code IDs**: Format `{jurisdiction}-muni-{chapter}-{section}` (future)

### Document Contents
- ID patterns with code locations for all 5 corpus types
- SQLite join SQL examples
- Cross-corpus join patterns (decision↔transcript, decision↔chunk)
- SQLite schema reference
- Design vs. actual format differences noted

### Files Changed
- `docs/critical/SQLITE_CHROMADB_JOIN_PATTERNS.md` (new)
- `pilot.json` (`sqlite_vector_join_patterns`: not_ready → ready)

**Progress:** 70/131 items ready (53%)

**Commit:** Session 277: Document SQLite-ChromaDB join patterns

## Session 278: UnifiedSearch class + cosine distance fix
Date: 2025-12-15

### Work Done
1. **Created UnifiedSearch class** (`packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/search/unified.py`):
   - `search_all()`: Multi-corpus semantic search across decisions, pdf, transcript, issue, municipal_code
   - `search_corpus()`: Single-corpus search with corpus-specific filters
   - `get_available_corpora()`: Registry returning CorpusInfo with availability and doc counts
   - `get_stats()`: Comprehensive statistics for all corpora
   - Returns `UnifiedSearchResult` objects (defined in Session 276)

2. **Fixed ChromaDB distance metric bug**:
   - Root cause: Collections were created without specifying distance metric
   - ChromaDB defaults to L2 (Euclidean), but code assumed cosine in `_results_to_search_results()`
   - Fix: Added `"hnsw:space": "cosine"` to all 5 `create_collection()` calls
   - Re-indexed all collections (decisions, chunks, issues, municipal_code)
   - Scores now correctly in [-1, 1] range (were -300+ before)

3. **Added search module**:
   - New `_internal/search/` directory with `__init__.py` and `unified.py`
   - Exports: `UnifiedSearch`, `CorpusInfo`, `CORPUS_TYPES`

4. **Wrote comprehensive tests** (`test_unified_search.py`):
   - 26 tests covering unit and integration scenarios
   - Tests for: corpus types, init, get_available_corpora, search_all, search_corpus, get_stats
   - Integration tests with real San Rafael vector index

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/search/__init__.py` (new)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/search/unified.py` (new - UnifiedSearch class)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/meetings/embeddings.py` (added hnsw:space cosine to 5 collections)
- `packages/civic/tests/test_unified_search.py` (new - 26 tests)
- `pilot.json` (3 items: unified_search_interface, result_merging, corpus_registry → ready)

## Session 280: Query Layer Integration - what_happened_full_context()
Date: 2025-12-16

### Work Done
1. **Implemented query layer integration for decision-transcript linking**:
   - Added `DecisionWithContext` and `TranscriptLink` dataclasses to `civic.civic`
   - Added `search_decisions_with_context()` function to `civic.history`
   - Added `what_happened_full_context()` method to `Civic` class
   - Uses semantic search to find transcript chunks related to each decision

2. **New API method**:
   ```python
   c = Civic("san-rafael")
   results = c.what_happened_full_context("homeless shelter", top_k=5)
   for r in results:
       print(f"{r.decision.title}: {r.decision.outcome}")
       if r.has_transcript:
           for link in r.public_comments:
               print(f"  Public: {link.text[:60]}...")
   ```

3. **Result types**:
   - `DecisionWithContext`: Decision + list of TranscriptLink + confidence
   - `TranscriptLink`: Transcript chunk with speaker, video_id, timestamps, video_url property
   - Properties: `has_transcript`, `public_comments`, `staff_discussion`, `council_discussion`

4. **Added tests**:
   - 7 new tests in test_queries.py (unit tests for dataclasses)
   - 3 new tests in test_civic.py (smoke tests for Civic method)
   - 4 new tests in test_integration_rag_san_rafael.py (integration tests with real data)

5. **Updated pilot.json**: `query_layer_integration` → ready

### Changes Made

   - Added `build_legislation_index()` method to load and index state bills from JSON

   - Creates searchable text combining bill name, summary, leverage point, and keywords
   - Stores metadata: bill_id, bill_name, topic, status, enacted, local_deadline, official_url

## Session 285 (continued) - 2025-12-16

**Item:** Data directory restructure (legislation/ + funding/)

### Summary
Restructured data directories for cleaner taxonomy:

**Old structure:**
- `data/legislative_context/california_{topic}.json` (state bills)
- `data/federal_programs/{topic}.json` (federal programs)

**New structure:**
- `data/legislation/state/california/{topic}.json` (state bills)
- `data/funding/federal/{topic}.json` (federal programs)

This separates "laws" (legislation/) from "money" (funding/) - matching user mental models.

- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/legislative/legislative_context_cache.py` - updated paths

- Data files moved to new locations

## Session 290: config_validation - Startup Configuration Validation
**Date:** 2025-12-17
**Phase:** Pilot
**Item:** `config_validation` (deployment_artifacts > configuration, P2)

### Summary
Implemented comprehensive configuration validation at startup with structured output, category validators, CLI command, and full test coverage.

### Implementation Details

#### 1. ValidationResult Dataclass
New structured result class for validation output:
- `passed: bool` - Overall validation status
- `errors: List[str]` - Blocking errors (must fix)
- `warnings: List[str]` - Non-blocking issues
- `suggestions: Dict[str, str]` - How to fix issues
- `format_errors()` - Detailed error output
- `format_summary()` - Concise status
- `to_dict()` - JSON serialization

#### 2. Category Validators
Enhanced `CivicConfig.validate_environment()` with 5 category validators:
- `_validate_core_config()` - CIVIC_ENV, CIVIC_API_PORT
- `_validate_security_config()` - CIVIC_WEB_KEY, CIVIC_CORS_ORIGINS, auth methods
- `_validate_data_paths()` - Bundled/user data directory existence and permissions
- `_validate_llm_config()` - OPENAI_API_KEY, model validation, placeholder detection
- `_validate_external_services()` - Optional API key placeholder detection

#### 3. Environment-Specific Validation
- **Production/Staging**: Strict validation (errors on missing required vars)
- **Development**: Lenient validation (warnings for missing optional vars)
- Detects placeholder values (`sk-proj-...`, `your-`, `AIza...`)
- Validates model names against known OpenAI models

#### 4. CLI Command
```bash
python -m civic_services.core.config [--json] [--strict]
```
- Human-readable output by default
- `--json` for structured output
- `--strict` treats warnings as errors
- Exit code 0 (pass) or 1 (fail)

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/core/config.py` - Enhanced validation
- `packages/civic/tests/test_config_validation.py` (NEW) - 26 tests
- `pilot.json` - Status: ready

### Test Results
- 26/26 config validation tests passing
- 34/34 smoke tests passing

### Next Session
Next P2 item: Check `pilot.json` for next not_ready item

**Commit:** Session 290: config_validation - Startup configuration validation with structured output

User requested reproducible backend ingestion for:
1. Knowledge transfer to app admins

Panel synthesis identified 5 perspectives:
- Data Engineer: Pipeline reliability, orchestration (Dagster/Prefect)

- Research Data Manager: Reproducibility, DOIs, versioning

Before adding items, verified against existing implementations:
- **EXISTS**: BaseExtractor protocol, Meeting/Decision/Issue dataclasses, civic-app-schema.json
- **EXISTS**: Legistar, CivicClerk, ProudCity extraction clients
- **PARTIAL**: batch_process_san_rafael_meetings.py (transcription only)

## Session 294

**Date:** 2025-12-17
**Item:** error_alerts (monitoring_observability > alerting)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented error rate monitoring and alerting system that:
- Monitors 5xx error rates from structured JSON logs
- Sends email alerts when thresholds exceeded (5% = elevated, 10% = critical)
- Integrates with /health endpoint to expose error metrics
- Debounces alerts to prevent notification storms (15 min cooldown)

### Implementation Details
- **ErrorMetricsCollector**: Reads logs/civic.json.log, calculates error rates over configurable time windows
- **ErrorAlertManager**: Manages thresholds, debouncing, and email notifications
- Thresholds: 5% error rate = elevated (degraded status), 10% = critical (unhealthy)
- Minimum 10 requests required before alerting (avoids noise in low-traffic periods)
- Alert history stored in data/error_alerts.json

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/monitoring/error_alerting.py` (NEW)
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/monitoring/__init__.py` (exports)
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/civic_api_integrated.py` (health endpoint integration)
- `pilot.json` - Updated error_alerts status to ready (88/153, 58%)

### /health Endpoint Integration
The error_rate check is now included in health response:
```json
{
  "checks": {
    "error_rate": {
      "status": "healthy|elevated|critical",
      "details": {
        "window_minutes": 5,
        "total_requests": N,
        "error_count": N,
        "error_rate_percent": X.X,
        "top_error_endpoints": [...]
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### Email Configuration
Alerts use existing SMTP config from .env.example:
- CIVIC_ALERT_EMAILS: Comma-separated recipient list
- CIVIC_SMTP_SERVER/PORT/USERNAME/PASSWORD: SMTP credentials

Without email config, alerts are logged only (log_only mode).

**Commit:** Session 294: error_alerts - Error rate monitoring and alerting

## Session 296: request_count + response_times - Request metrics monitoring
**Date:** 2025-12-17
**Item:** request_count, response_times (monitoring_observability > metrics)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented request volume and response time tracking to monitor API health.
The RequestMetricsManager reads structured JSON logs and calculates:
- Total request counts and requests per minute
- Response time percentiles (p50, p95, p99) and averages
- Breakdown by HTTP method and endpoint
- Status code distribution (2xx/3xx/4xx/5xx)

### Implementation

1. **RequestMetricsCollector** (`request_metrics.py`)
   - Reads `request_complete` events from `logs/civic.json.log`
   - Configurable time windows (default 5 minutes)
   - Path normalization for aggregation (removes query strings, replaces IDs)
   - Efficient log parsing with time-windowed filtering

2. **Request completion logging** (`civic_api_integrated.py`)
   - Added `_request_start_time` and `_request_method` instance vars to do_GET/POST/PUT/DELETE
   - Added `_log_request_complete()` helper method
   - Calls `log_request_complete()` from send_json, send_auth_error, send_rate_limit_error
   - Logs method, path, status_code, duration_ms for every response

3. **Health endpoint integration**
   - Added `get_request_metrics_manager()` lazy singleton
   - Added `_check_request_metrics()` health check method
   - Exposed at `/health` and `/api/status` under `checks.request_metrics`
   - Shows: total_requests, requests_per_minute, response times, top endpoints

4. **Tests** (`test_request_metrics.py`)
   - 12 tests covering collector, manager, singleton, and health integration
   - Tests path normalization, percentile calculation, endpoint aggregation

### Files Created
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/monitoring/request_metrics.py`
- `packages/civic/tests/test_request_metrics.py`

### Files Modified

## Session 297: active_users - Track unique users per day
**Date:** 2025-12-17
**Item:** active_users (monitoring_observability > metrics)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented active users tracking to monitor unique users per time window and daily active users (DAU). The system tracks users by:
- Authenticated user_id (from Bearer tokens) for authenticated requests
- Client IP address for anonymous requests

### Implementation Details
1. **ActiveUsersMetrics dataclass** - Stores metrics including:
   - unique_users: Total unique users in time window
   - active_users_per_hour: Rate projection
   - authenticated_users: Users with Bearer tokens
   - anonymous_users: IP-only users
   - daily_active_users: 24h unique users

2. **ActiveUsersCollector** - Parses structured JSON logs for:
   - request_start events (has client_ip)
   - request_complete events (when client_ip is present)

3. **ActiveUsersManager** - High-level interface with:
   - get_active_users() for /health endpoint
   - get_unique_users_count() convenience method
   - get_daily_active_users() convenience method

4. **Health endpoint integration** - Added `active_users` check to serve_status()
   - Exposes at /health and /api/status under checks.active_users
   - Shows: unique_users, active_users_per_hour, authenticated vs anonymous breakdown, daily_active_users

### Files Created
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/monitoring/active_users.py`
- `packages/civic/tests/test_active_users.py`

### Files Modified
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/civic_api_integrated.py`
- `pilot.json`

## Session 298: gap_detection - Compare ingested count vs source count
**Date:** 2025-12-17
**Item:** gap_detection (ingestion_visibility > cli_status)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented gap detection for the `civic status` CLI to compare ingested counts against source API counts. This helps operators identify when data ingestion has fallen behind the source.

### What Was Implemented
1. **get_source_counts()** - Queries external APIs to get source counts:
   - ProudCity for meetings (city-san-rafael specific)
   - SeeClickFix for issues (paginated fetching with safety limit)
   - Graceful error handling for offline sources

2. **calculate_gaps()** - Compares ingested vs source counts:
   - Returns gap (source - ingested)
   - Calculates coverage percentage
   - Handles errors gracefully (shows "source unavailable")
   - Overall coverage aggregation

3. **Gap Analysis CLI output** - Added new section to `civic status`:
   - Shows ingested/source counts with coverage percentage
   - Color-coded indicators: OK (>95%), GAP (80-95%), LOW (<80%)
   - Handles >100% coverage (historical data) gracefully
   - Overall coverage summary with "(includes historical)" note

4. **--check-gaps CLI flag** - Optional flag to enable gap detection
   - Disabled by default (queries external APIs)
   - Works with both human-readable and --json output

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic/src/civic/cli.py` - Added gap detection functions and CLI flag
- `packages/civic/tests/test_cli_status.py` - Added 10 new tests for gap detection
- `pilot.json` - Updated gap_detection status to "ready"

### Example Output
```
Gap Analysis (ingested vs source):
  meetings        : 0/115 | 0.0% coverage | gap: 115 [LOW]
  issues          : 1,340/100 | 100.0% coverage | (+1,240 extra) [OK]

  Overall coverage: 100.0% (includes historical)
```

### Tests
- 10 new tests covering calculate_gaps, get_source_counts, and print_status with gaps
- All 40 CLI status tests passing

**Commit:** Session 298: gap_detection - Compare ingested count vs source count

## Session 299: admin_status_endpoint

**Goal:** GET /admin/status returns JSON pipeline health

### Implementation
- Added `/admin/status` route to `do_GET` in civic_api_integrated.py:688-690
- Implemented `serve_admin_status()` method (civic_api_integrated.py:7318-7511)
- Returns comprehensive JSON including:
  - Database table stats (meetings, agenda_items, issues, initiatives) with counts and timestamps
  - ChromaDB collection stats (document counts per collection)
  - File size stats (state_db, participation_db)
  - Overall pipeline health status (healthy/degraded/unhealthy)
- Supports `?jurisdiction=` query parameter (default: san-rafael)

### Example Response
```json
{
  "status": "healthy",
  "timestamp": "2024-12-17T21:53:00.000000Z",
  "jurisdiction": "san-rafael",
  "database": {
    "meetings": {"count": 2, "earliest": "...", "latest": "...", "last_updated": "..."},
    "agenda_items": {"count": 1, "last_enriched": "..."},
    "issues": {"count": 2, "by_status": {"open": 1, "closed": 1}},
    "initiatives": {"count": 1, "last_updated": "..."},
    "status": "connected"
  },
  "chromadb": {
    "collections": {"decisions": {...}, "chunks": {...}, ...},
    "total_documents": 1000,
    "status": "connected"
  }
}
```

## Session 313: generalized_pipeline_runner

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** generalized_pipeline_runner (city_onboarding > orchestration)
**Status:** COMPLETE

### Summary
Implemented a generalized Pipeline class for ETL orchestration with three stages:
discover → ingest → index, each with status callbacks for dashboard consumption.

### Implementation
- **Pipeline class**: Orchestrates three-stage ETL workflow
- **StageStatus/StageState**: Track per-stage state, items, duration, errors
- **PipelineResult**: Full result after run() completes
- **Status callbacks**: on_stage_start, on_stage_progress, on_stage_complete, on_error
- **pipeline.status()**: Dashboard-consumable JSON dict for real-time monitoring

### Key Features
- Three stages matching dashboard visualization: Available → Ingested → Searchable
- Status callbacks for real-time progress reporting
- Graceful error handling with per-stage error accumulation
- Skip capability (skip_index=True for testing)
- Reset method for re-running pipelines
- IndexTarget protocol for pluggable indexing backends

### Tests Added

## Session 316: storage_backend_protocol

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** storage_backend_protocol (city_onboarding > orchestration)
**Status:** COMPLETE

### Summary
Created StorageBackend and VectorBackend protocols for the 4-stage pipeline:
discover → ingest → store → index

### Implementation

**StorageBackend Protocol** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py`):
- `@runtime_checkable` protocol for primary data persistence (SQLite, Postgres)
- Methods: `validate()`, `store_meetings()`, `get_meetings()`, `get_stats()`, `delete_meetings()`
- Dataclasses: `StorageStats`, `StorageValidationResult`
- Supports temporal versioning via `as_of` parameter

**VectorBackend Protocol** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/vector.py`):
- `@runtime_checkable` protocol for vector search (ChromaDB, pgvector)
- Methods: `validate()`, `index_from_storage()`, `search()`, `get_stats()`, `delete_index()`
- Dataclasses: `SearchResult`, `VectorStats`, `VectorValidationResult`
- Key design: `index_from_storage()` reads from StorageBackend, not memory

**Key Design Principle:**
Index reads from storage, not memory. This ensures:
- Data is persisted before indexing
- Re-indexing possible without re-fetching from source
- Clear separation of store and index stages

### Files Created
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/__init__.py` - Module exports
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py` - StorageBackend protocol
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/vector.py` - VectorBackend protocol
- `packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py` - 18 tests

### Tests
- 18 storage protocol tests passing
- 34 smoke tests passing

### Next Steps
These protocols enable:
1. SQLiteBackend implementation (wraps StateManager)
2. ChromaDBBackend implementation (wraps VectorStore)
3. Pipeline extension to use store stage between ingest and index

**Commit:** Session 316: storage_backend_protocol - StorageBackend + VectorBackend protocols

## Session 324: transcription_cron - civic-extract transcribe CLI

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** transcription_cron (pipeline_automation > scheduling)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created `civic-extract transcribe` CLI for automated audio transcription using AssemblyAI with speaker diarization. Follows the pattern established by audio.py.

### Implementation

**New CLI:** `civic-extract transcribe`

Features:
- One-time run: `civic-extract transcribe --jurisdiction city-san-rafael`
- Scheduled mode: `--schedule` runs daily at 9am (after audio at 8am)
- Dry-run mode: `--dry-run` shows files that would be transcribed with cost estimate
- Limit mode: `--limit N` transcribes only N files
- Checkpoint support: Saves/resumes from `data/checkpoints/transcribe_{jurisdiction}.json`
- Skip already-transcribed: Checks `data/testimony/testimony_{video_id}.json`
- Configurable speakers: `--min-speakers`, `--max-speakers` for diarization tuning

### Usage
```bash
civic-extract transcribe --jurisdiction city-san-rafael                    # Transcribe all pending
civic-extract transcribe --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --dry-run          # Show cost estimate
civic-extract transcribe --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --limit 1          # Transcribe one file
civic-extract transcribe --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --schedule         # Start 9am scheduler
```

### Test Results
Successfully tested with San Rafael:
- Transcribed 2 audio files (zHPgE8sZNRs: 82.6 min, k5ZUhxHn5pE: 2.6 min)
- 7 speakers, 40 utterances in test video

### Summary

### Implementation

**New CLI:** `civic-extract seeclickfix`

Features:
- One-time run: `civic-extract seeclickfix --jurisdiction city-san-rafael`

## Session 327: missed_run_alerts - Pipeline Monitor CLI

**Date:** 2025-12-21
**Item:** missed_run_alerts (pipeline_automation > heartbeat_monitoring)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created `civic-extract monitor` CLI command to detect missed/overdue pipeline runs by checking checkpoint timestamps.

### Implementation

**New CLI:** `civic-extract monitor`

Features:
- Check all pipelines: `civic-extract monitor --check-all`
- Single pipeline: `civic-extract monitor --pipeline discover`
- Override threshold: `--max-age 24` (hours)
- JSON output: `--format json` for dashboard integration
- CI mode: `--exit-on-overdue` returns exit code 1 if any overdue

### Pipeline Configurations
| Pipeline | Schedule | Alert Threshold |
|----------|----------|-----------------|
| discover | Daily 6am | >30h |
| youtube | Daily 7am | >30h |
| audio | Daily 8am | >30h |
| seeclickfix | Daily 8am | >30h |
| transcribe | Daily 9am | >30h |
| legislative | Weekly Sun 6am | >192h (8d) |

### Usage
```bash
# Check all pipelines
civic-extract monitor --check-all

# Check specific pipeline with custom threshold
civic-extract monitor --pipeline discover --max-age 24

# JSON output for dashboards
civic-extract monitor --check-all --format json

# CI/cron integration (exit 1 if overdue)
civic-extract monitor --check-all --exit-on-overdue
```

### Files Created/Modified
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/monitor.py` - New monitor command
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/__init__.py` - Registered monitor subcommand

### Also Marked Ready
During review, discovered these items were already implemented:
- `success_logging` - Checkpoint files contain timestamps and counts
- `last_processed_tracking` - Checkpoints store last_video_id, last_meeting_id, etc.

## Session 337: storage_backend_protocol - 4-Stage Pipeline Implementation

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** storage_backend_protocol (city_onboarding > orchestration)
**Status:** Complete

### Summary
Implemented the critical storage backend protocol gap identified in Session 336's ETL audit. The pipeline now has a proper 4-stage pattern: discover → ingest → store → index, with data persisted before indexing.

### Key Files Created/Modified

**New Files:**
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py` - SQLiteBackend implementing StorageBackend protocol
- `packages/civic/tests/test_sqlite_backend.py` - 26 comprehensive tests

**Modified Files:**
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/__init__.py` - Export SQLiteBackend
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/pipeline.py` - Add store stage, update index to read from storage
- `pilot.json` - Mark 4 items as ready

### Implementation Details

1. **SQLiteBackend Class** (`sqlite_backend.py:17-537`)
   - `backend_type` property returns "sqlite"
   - `validate()` - Schema and connectivity check
   - `store_meetings()` - Atomic storage with temporal versioning
   - `get_meetings()` - Retrieval with temporal and date filtering
   - `get_stats()` - StorageStats with counts and date ranges
   - `delete_meetings()` - Soft delete with temporal versioning

2. **Pipeline Store Stage** (`pipeline.py:554-607`)
   - New `_run_store()` method
   - Calls `storage_target.store_meetings()`
   - Backward compatible - works without storage_target (in-memory fallback)
   - Pipeline.STAGES = ["discover", "ingest", "store", "index"]

3. **Index Reads From Storage** (`pipeline.py:626-657`)
   - `_run_index()` now prefers `storage_target.get_meetings()`
   - Falls back to `_ingested_meetings` if no storage_target
   - Metadata tracks source ("storage_backend" or "in_memory")

### Test Results

- 26 new tests in test_sqlite_backend.py: 26 passed
- 18 existing protocol tests: 18 passed  
- 34 smoke tests: 34 passed

## Session 340: postgres_backend_implementation - PostgreSQL Storage Backend

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** postgres_backend_implementation (city_onboarding > orchestration)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created PostgresBackend class implementing the StorageBackend protocol. This proves the storage abstraction works for multiple backend implementations, enabling municipalities with existing PostgreSQL infrastructure to adopt Civic.

### Implementation

1. **PostgresBackend Class** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py`):
   - Full implementation of StorageBackend protocol (558 lines)
   - All 5 protocol methods: validate, store_meetings, get_meetings, get_stats, delete_meetings
   - Uses psycopg2 with RealDictCursor for dict-like row access
   - Temporal versioning via composite PK (id, valid_from) and valid_to column
   - Same schema as SQLiteBackend for data portability
   - `backend_type` property returns "postgres"
   - `get_stats()` includes `metadata={"backend_type": "postgres"}`
   - Database size via `pg_database_size(current_database())`

2. **Conditional Import Pattern**:
   - psycopg2 is optional - import fails gracefully with clear error message
   - `PSYCOPG2_AVAILABLE` flag controls runtime checks
   - Allows package to work without psycopg2 when only SQLite is needed

3. **Test Suite** (`packages/civic/tests/test_postgres_backend.py`):
   - 28 tests mirroring SQLiteBackend test structure
   - Tests skipped via `pytestmark` when CIVIC_TEST_POSTGRES_URL not set
   - Includes protocol compliance, CRUD operations, temporal versioning, Pipeline integration

4. **Package Export** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/__init__.py`):
   - Added PostgresBackend to public API
   - Available via `from civic.storage import PostgresBackend`

### Key Design Decisions

1. **Same Schema**: Both SQLite and Postgres use identical table structures:
   - city_states, meetings, agenda_items
   - Composite PKs for temporal versioning
   - Same indexes for query performance

2. **Connection-per-Request**: No connection pooling yet (suitable for pilot scale)

3. **ISO 8601 Datetime Strings**: Cross-backend compatibility for temporal queries

4. **Soft Deletes**: Temporal versioning means no data loss on delete

### Testing
- 34 smoke tests: passed
- 44 SQLite/protocol tests: passed  
- 28 Postgres tests: skipped (expected - no DB configured)

### Why This Matters
1. **Proves modularity**: StorageBackend protocol works with multiple implementations
2. **Open-source adoption**: Municipalities with Postgres get zero-code integration
3. **Production readiness**: Postgres better suited for multi-user deployments
4. **Config-only migration**: Switch backends via environment variable

### Pilot Progress
- Before: 142/168 items (84.5%)
- After: 143/168 items (85.1%)
- 1 item completed: postgres_backend_implementation

### Next Steps
Need to assign a new P0 before running /nextsesh.

## Session 343: operations_backend_protocol - Add Operation Tracking to StorageBackend Protocol

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** operations_backend_protocol (admin_operations > operation_status)

### Summary
Added operation tracking methods to the StorageBackend protocol, enabling consistent operation tracking across SQLite (dev) and PostgreSQL (production) backends. Session 341 had added operations to StateManager (SQLite-only); this session promotes them to the protocol layer.

### Implementation

1. **Protocol Definition** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py:219-327`):
   - Added 5 operation methods to StorageBackend protocol:
     - `create_operation(operation_id, jurisdiction_id, name)` → Dict
     - `update_operation_status(operation_id, status, current_step?, progress_percent?, items_processed?, items_total?)` → bool
     - `complete_operation(operation_id, result, error?)` → bool
     - `get_operation(operation_id)` → Optional[Dict]
     - `get_operations(jurisdiction_id?, status?, limit?)` → List[Dict]

2. **PostgresBackend Implementation** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py:593-863`):
   - Added operations table schema in `_ensure_schema()` (lines 244-276)
   - Implemented all 5 operation methods with proper psycopg2 parameterized queries
   - Updated protocol compliance check to include operation methods

3. **SQLiteBackend Implementation** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py:581-847`):
   - Added operations table schema in `_ensure_schema()` (lines 231-263)
   - Implemented all 5 operation methods with proper sqlite3 queries
   - All methods ensure schema exists before querying

4. **Tests** (`packages/civic/tests/test_sqlite_backend.py:312-509`):
   - Added `TestSQLiteBackendOperations` class with 14 tests covering:
     - create_operation, get_operation, get_operation_not_found
     - update_operation_status (full and partial updates)
     - complete_operation (success and failure)
     - get_operations (all, by_jurisdiction, by_status, with_limit)
     - Full workflow test

5. **Protocol Tests** (`packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py`):
   - Updated mock implementations to include operation methods
   - All protocol compliance tests pass

### Testing
```bash
pytest packages/civic/tests/test_sqlite_backend.py -v  # 38 passed
pytest packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py -v  # 18 passed
pytest packages/civic/tests/test_civic.py -q  # 34 passed
```

### Operations Table Schema
```sql
CREATE TABLE operations (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    jurisdiction_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',  -- pending, running, completed, failed
    started_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
    completed_at TIMESTAMP,
    result_json TEXT,
    error TEXT,
    duration_seconds REAL,
    current_step TEXT,
    progress_percent REAL DEFAULT 0,
    items_processed INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    items_total INTEGER DEFAULT 0
)
```

## Session 345: data_path_resolver - Centralize File Path Generation

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** data_path_resolver (data_architecture > configuration_management)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created `DataPathResolver` class to centralize all data file paths with environment variable support, unblocking containerized deployment.

### Problem
64 hardcoded path references across 42 files:
- `data/civic_state.db` (31 instances)
- `data/pilot/vectors/` (16 instances)
- `data/checkpoints/` (17 instances)

### Implementation

1. **DataPathResolver Class** (`packages/civic/src/civic/paths.py`, 250 lines):
   - Supports environment variables:
     - `CIVIC_DATA_ROOT`: Unified root (development)
     - `CIVIC_BUNDLED_DATA_DIR`: Read-only data (production)
     - `CIVIC_USER_DATA_DIR`: Persistent data (production)
   - Convenience methods: `state_db()`, `vectors_dir()`, `checkpoints_dir()`, `events_dir()`, `legislation_dir()`
   - Module-level functions: `get_state_db_path()`, `get_vectors_dir()`, `get_bundled_path()`, `get_user_path()`
   - Singleton pattern with `reset_resolver()` for testing

2. **Refactored Key Files in civic package**:
   - `civic.py`: Uses `get_state_db_path()` for default db_path
   - `sqlite_backend.py`: Uses `get_state_db_path()` in constructor
   - `_internal/state/manager.py`: Uses `get_state_db_path()`
   - `_internal/meetings/embeddings.py`: Uses `get_vectors_dir()`, `get_state_db_path()`

3. **Refactored civic-extraction** (standalone package):
   - `pipeline.py`: Added `_get_data_root()` helper reading `CIVIC_DATA_ROOT`
   - `checkpoint_path_for_jurisdiction()`: Now uses env var with fallback

4. **Tests** (`test_paths.py`, 36 tests):
   - Environment variable configuration
   - Bundled vs user data separation
   - Production (Fly.io) volume simulation
   - Backwards compatibility (string return types)

**Files Changed:**

## Session 347: error_logs - Add Detailed Error Logging for Operations

**Date:** 2025-12-22
**Item:** error_logs (admin_operations > operation_status)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Enhanced operation error handling to capture detailed error information (error type, stack trace, timestamp) in result_json for dashboard display. This enables administrators to debug failed operations without needing server logs.

### Implementation

1. **Main Error Handler** (`civic_api_integrated.py:7842-7859`):
   - Captures `error_type` (e.g., ValueError, HTTPError)
   - Captures `error_traceback` (full stack trace)
   - Captures `failed_at` timestamp
   - Stores all in result_json via `complete_operation()`
