## Session 356 (2025-12-23)

### Task: research_abstraction

Created extensible research infrastructure by extracting generic orchestration from housing-specific code.

### Changes

1. **Created `base.py`** - `BaseResearcher` abstract class with:
   - Common orchestration: `research()`, `research_ensemble()`
   - Rate limiting and parallel query execution
   - Audit trail persistence
   - Municipality config loading
   - Utility methods: `_slugify()`, `_generate_keywords()`, `_extract_relevant_text()`

2. **Refactored `funding.py`** - `MunicipalFundingResearcher(BaseResearcher)`:
   - Implements abstract methods for housing-specific logic
   - `_get_topic()` → returns "housing"
   - `_build_prompt()` → housing-specific structured format
   - `_parse_response()` → MunicipalFundingPrograms parsing
   - `_merge_results()` → program deduplication and merging
   - Backward-compatible API: same `research()` and `research_ensemble()` signatures

3. **Updated `__init__.py`** - Export `BaseResearcher` for extensibility

### Architecture

```
research/
├── __init__.py          # Export BaseResearcher + MunicipalFundingResearcher
├── base.py              # NEW: BaseResearcher abstract class
├── providers/           # Unchanged
└── municipal/
    ├── funding.py       # MunicipalFundingResearcher(BaseResearcher)
    ├── schemas.py       # Housing-specific schemas
    └── query_templates.py
```

### Extensibility Pattern

```python
from civic_extraction.research import BaseResearcher

class TransportationResearcher(BaseResearcher):
    def _get_topic(self) -> str:
        return "transportation"
    
    def _get_query_templates(self) -> list[QueryTemplate]:
        return TRANSPORTATION_TEMPLATES
    
    def _build_prompt(self, jurisdiction, state, **kwargs) -> str:
        return "..."
    
    def _parse_response(self, result) -> Optional[BaseModel]:
        return TransportationPrograms(...)
    
    def _merge_results(self, result) -> Optional[BaseModel]:
        return merged_data
```

   - `build_chunks_index_from_sql(storage_backend)` - reads chunks from SQL, creates ChromaDB collection

   - Batch processing (100 chunks at a time) for memory efficiency

- Same temporal versioning pattern (valid_from/valid_to)

### Completed

  - Implemented `store_decisions()`, `get_decisions()`, `get_decision_count()`
  - Implemented `store_chunks()`, `get_chunks()`, `get_chunk_count()`
  - Created `get_storage_backend()` factory function in civic.storage

  - Installed psycopg2-binary dependency
  - Added 11 new tests for factory function and PostgresBackend structure

### Changes Made
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py` - Added decisions/chunks tables and methods
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/__init__.py` - Added get_storage_backend() factory
- `packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py` - Added 11 tests
- `.env.example` - Added DATABASE_URL documentation
- `pilot.json` - Marked postgres_backend ready

### Architecture Decision
PostgresBackend now has full parity with SQLiteBackend for:
- Meetings (store/get/delete)
- Operations (create/update/complete/get)
- Decisions (store/get/count)
- Chunks (store/get/count)

The `get_storage_backend()` factory allows transparent backend selection via DATABASE_URL.

## Session 370: blob_storage_abstraction - BlobStorage Protocol for Large Files

**Date:** 2025-12-26
**Item:** blob_storage_abstraction (deployment_artifacts > cloud_storage)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created BlobStorage protocol and implementations for storing large binary files (PDFs, audio, transcripts) outside the database. This complements StorageBackend (structured data) with blob-optimized storage.

### Implementation

1. **BlobStorage Protocol** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/blob.py`):
   - `BlobStorage`: Protocol for binary object storage with upload/download/list/delete
   - `BlobStats`: Statistics dataclass (total_objects, total_bytes, by_content_type)
   - `BlobValidationResult`: Preflight validation result (connected, writable, errors)

2. **LocalBlobBackend** (development):
   - Filesystem-based storage for local development
   - Creates nested directory structures automatically
   - Stores metadata in memory (content types)

3. **R2Backend** (production):
   - Cloudflare R2 (S3-compatible) using boto3
   - 10GB free tier with zero egress fees
   - Factory methods: `from_env()` and `from_url()`
   - Full CRUD operations via S3 API

4. **Factory Function**:
   - `get_blob_storage(url)`: Auto-selects backend based on URL scheme
   - `r2://` → R2Backend
   - `local://` → LocalBlobBackend
   - Default → LocalBlobBackend("data/blobs")

5. **Environment Configuration** (`.env.example`):
   - `BLOB_STORAGE_URL`: Storage URL (r2:// or local://)
   - `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`: R2 API token access key
   - `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`: R2 API token secret

### Tests Added
24 new tests in `test_storage_protocols.py`:
- BlobStats dataclass tests (3)
- BlobValidationResult tests (3)
- BlobStorage protocol tests (2)
- LocalBlobBackend tests (9)
- R2Backend structure tests (4)
- get_blob_storage factory tests (7)

### Architecture Pattern
Mirrors the existing StorageBackend pattern:
- Protocol defines interface
- Multiple implementations (Local, R2)
- Factory function for environment-based selection
- Dataclasses for stats and validation

### Changes Made
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/blob.py` - New file: BlobStorage protocol + implementations
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/__init__.py` - Added exports for blob types
- `.env.example` - Added BLOB_STORAGE section
- `packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py` - Added 24 blob storage tests
- `pilot.json` - Marked blob_storage_abstraction as ready

### Progress
- Before: 172/191 items ready (90.1%)
- After: 173/191 items ready (90.6%)

## Session 374: storage_migration_script - Cloud Storage Migration Tool

**Date:** 2025-12-26
**Item:** storage_migration_script (deployment_artifacts > cloud_storage)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Created a storage migration script to promote local development data to cloud storage for pilot deployment. The script handles SQLite→PostgreSQL, Local→R2 blob, and ChromaDB→Fly.io volume migrations.

### Implementation

1. **Migration Script** (`scripts/migrate_storage.py`):
   - Full CLI with argparse: `--dry-run`, `--verbose`, `--only`, `--verify`
   - Environment variable support: TARGET_POSTGRES_URL, TARGET_R2_URL
   - MigrationManifest class for idempotent resume and audit trails
   - StorageMigration orchestrator class

2. **Database Migration (SQLite → PostgreSQL)**:
   - Uses existing StorageBackend protocol implementations
   - Migrates: meetings, decisions, chunks
   - Atomic transactions per jurisdiction
   - Count verification after migration

3. **Blob Migration (Local → R2)**:
   - Uses existing BlobStorage protocol implementations
   - Skips already-migrated files (idempotent)
   - Content type inference from file extensions
   - Progress reporting for large migrations

4. **Vector Migration (ChromaDB → Fly.io Volume)**:
   - Generates deployment instructions for Fly.io volume mount
   - Calculates storage requirements
   - Documents alternative re-indexing approach

5. **Features**:
   - Dry-run mode: Shows what would be migrated without changes
   - Progress reporting with percentages
   - Manifest persistence for resume capability
   - Source/target validation before migration
   - Post-migration verification

### Key Files
- `scripts/migrate_storage.py` - Migration script (450+ lines)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py` - PostgreSQL implementation (fully implemented)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/blob.py` - R2/Local blob implementations

### Usage Examples
```bash
# Dry run (show what would be migrated)
python scripts/migrate_storage.py --dry-run

# Full migration
python scripts/migrate_storage.py --target-postgres "postgresql://..." --target-r2 "r2://..."

# Migrate specific data type
python scripts/migrate_storage.py --only database

# Verify migration
python scripts/migrate_storage.py --verify
```

### Progress
- Before: 176/191 items ready (92%)
- After: 177/191 items ready (92%)

## Session 384 (2025-12-27): vector_indexing_cloud

### What Was Done
- Created `vectors.py` CLI command for cloud vector indexing
- Indexes chunks/decisions from Postgres into pgvector
- Supports multiple corpus types: `--corpus chunks/decisions/meetings/all`
- Stats mode: `--stats` shows index coverage
- Dry-run mode: `--dry-run` shows indexing plan
- Test search: `--test-search "query"` runs semantic search
- Reindex: `--reindex` forces rebuild

### Usage
```bash
# Index chunks into pgvector
civic-extract vectors --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --corpus chunks

# Check statistics
civic-extract vectors --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --stats

# Dry run to see plan
civic-extract vectors --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --dry-run

# Test semantic search
civic-extract vectors --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --test-search "housing development"
```

### Update: chunks_e2e_cloud reverted - found HTML extraction bug

Investigation revealed that current chunks are extracted from HTML meeting pages, not actual PDFs:
- `agenda_url` in database contains meeting page URLs (https://www.cityofsanrafael.org/meetings/...)
- These are HTML pages (~90KB), not PDF files
- Each meeting page has 10-24 PDF links (staff reports, agenda packet, minutes)
- Real agenda packets are 1-15MB

**Added degenerate case detection to chunks CLI:**
- `validate_pdf_content()` checks: Content-Type header, PDF magic bytes (%PDF-), file size
- `download_and_validate_pdf()` returns validation result with warnings
- Extraction now fails fast with clear error when HTML is downloaded instead of PDF

**Created new P0: chunks_pdf_extraction_fix**
- Need to parse HTML meeting pages to find actual PDF links
- Download and process each PDF (agenda packet + staff reports)
- Re-run extraction after fix

**Reverted:**
- chunks_e2e_cloud -> not_ready (blocked by chunks_pdf_extraction_fix)
- transcripts_e2e_cloud -> P1 (deferred)

## Session 394 - 2025-12-29

### Completed: chunks_extraction_reliability (P0)

Fixed PDF parsing reliability issues that were causing extraction to hang on large PDFs.

**Changes:**
- Added `_parse_pdf_with_timeout()` using `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor`
- Default 5 minute timeout per PDF, configurable via `--timeout` CLI flag
- Added `PDFParseTimeoutError` exception for graceful timeout handling
- Extraction continues to next meeting on timeout (logs error, doesn't hang)

**Results:**
- 23MB PDFs now parse in ~24 seconds (was hanging indefinitely)
- Full extraction completes in ~2 minutes (was 2+ hours)
- 34/46 meetings now have chunks in cloud
- 12 meetings without chunks are cancelled/closed sessions (no PDFs)

**Key file:** `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/chunks.py:627-672`

## Session 397: etl_cost_provenance - Centralized ETL Cost Tracking

**Date:** 2025-12-29
**Item:** etl_cost_provenance (monitoring_observability > cost_tracking)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Implemented centralized cost tracking for ETL pipelines before running the $46 transcription job. Costs are now recorded in the `etl_costs` Postgres table with full audit trail.

### Implementation

1. **Added etl_costs table to PostgresBackend** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py`):
   - Schema: `etl_costs(id, pipeline, jurisdiction_id, run_date, items_processed, cost_usd, duration_seconds, notes)`
   - Foreign key to `city_states.jurisdiction_id`
   - Indexes on jurisdiction_id, pipeline, run_date

2. **Added cost tracking methods** (`postgres_backend.py:2326-2482`):
   - `store_etl_cost(pipeline, jurisdiction_id, items_processed, cost_usd, duration_seconds, notes)` → returns cost_id
   - `get_etl_costs(jurisdiction_id, pipeline, limit)` → returns list of cost records
   - `get_etl_cost_summary(jurisdiction_id, pipeline)` → returns aggregate {total_cost_usd, total_items, run_count}

3. **Integrated with transcribe.py** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/transcribe.py:758-779`):
   - After successful transcriptions, calls `backend.store_etl_cost()` to record the run
   - Logs the cost record ID for audit trail

### Verification
```python
from civic.storage import get_storage_backend
backend = get_storage_backend()
cost_id = backend.store_etl_cost("test", "city-san-rafael", 1, 0.01)
# Returns: 1
costs = backend.get_etl_costs(pipeline="test")
# Returns: [{'id': 1, 'pipeline': 'test', 'jurisdiction_id': 'city-san-rafael', ...}]
summary = backend.get_etl_cost_summary(jurisdiction_id="city-san-rafael")
# Returns: {'total_cost_usd': 0.01, 'total_items': 1, 'run_count': 1}
```

### Next P0: transcripts_e2e_cloud
- Run: civic-extract transcribe --jurisdiction city-san-rafael --cloud
- Estimated cost: ~$46 total (~18 remaining × $2.40 avg per video)
- Cost tracking now in place to monitor spend

## Session 398: ETL Cost Tracking All Pipelines
**Date**: 2025-12-29
**P0 Completed**: etl_cost_tracking_all_pipelines

### Summary
Implemented proper ETL cost tracking across all pipelines using centralized MODEL_REGISTRY pricing.

### Changes Made

1. **model_registry.py** - Added `calculate_cost(model, usage)` function
   - Uses MODEL_REGISTRY pricing data (cost_per_1m_tokens)
   - Calculates actual costs from token usage returned by providers
   - Single source of truth for all pricing

2. **agenda_integration.py** - Added cumulative cost tracking
   - `_total_cost` and `_total_tokens` instance variables
   - `_call_llm()` now tracks cost after each call
   - `total_cost` and `total_tokens` properties
   - `reset_cost_tracking()` method
   - RetrospectiveAnalyzer inherits all cost tracking

3. **cli/decisions.py** - Uses actual costs from analyzer
   - Creates shared analyzer instance for all meetings
   - Passes analyzer to `extract_decisions_from_meeting()`
   - Records actual cost from `analyzer.total_cost`

4. **cli/research.py** - Already had actual costs from Perplexity
   - Added `store_etl_cost()` calls for both single and ensemble modes
   - Uses `result.raw_response.cost` and `result.total_cost`

5. **cli/vectors.py** - Records $0 cost (local embeddings)
   - Documents pipeline in ETL costs for full pipeline visibility
   - Uses local sentence-transformers (no API cost)

### Architecture
```
MODEL_REGISTRY (pricing)  ->  calculate_cost()  ->  AgendaIntegrator._call_llm()
                                                          |
                                                          v
                                              _total_cost accumulator
                                                          |
                                                          v
                                              store_etl_cost() in CLI
```

## Session 405 - 2025-12-29

### Strategic pivot: Remote compute for portable vector ETL

**Started:** vectors_e2e_cloud (P0)
**Outcome:** Pivoted to remote embedding support before indexing

### Analysis
- Current local embedding (nomic-embed-text-v1.5) requires sentence-transformers installed everywhere
- Not portable for CI/CD, GitHub Actions, or scheduled refresh jobs
- ~8,839 documents need indexing across 6 corpus types

### Decision
Add configurable embedding provider support:
- `--provider local` (default, dev)
- `--provider openai` (production, portable)
- `--provider voyage` (alternative)

Cost estimate: ~$0.09 for full corpus with OpenAI text-embedding-3-small

### Files changed
- pilot.json (vectors_e2e_cloud updated for remote compute requirement)
- docs/next_session_prompt.md (detailed implementation context)

### Next P0
vectors_e2e_cloud - Implement remote embedding provider abstraction

## Session 409: Content Hashing for Data Integrity

**Date:** 2025-12-30
**Items:** transcript_content_hash, chunk_content_hash, decision_content_hash (data_integrity > content_hashing)
**Priority:** P2

### Summary
Implemented SHA-256 content hashing for transcripts, chunks, and decisions stored in PostgresBackend. This enables verification that stored content hasn't been modified - foundational infrastructure for federation, legal defense, and anti-tampering.

### Implementation

1. **Integrity utility module** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/integrity.py`):
   - `compute_content_hash(content)`: Base SHA-256 hashing for str, bytes, dict, list
   - `compute_transcript_hash(transcript)`: Hash full transcript dict including utterances
   - `compute_chunk_hash(text)`: Hash extracted PDF text
   - `compute_decision_hash(decision)`: Hash canonical decision fields (excludes metadata)
   - `verify_content_hash(content, expected_hash)`: Verify content against stored hash
   - Handles None/empty input gracefully (returns None)
   - Uses sorted keys for dict consistency

2. **PostgresBackend updates** (`postgres_backend.py`):
   - `store_transcripts()`: Computes and stores content_hash from transcript dict
   - `store_chunks()`: Computes and stores content_hash from text field
   - `store_decisions()`: Computes and stores content_hash from canonical fields
   - Hash columns already exist via migration 015_data_integrity.sql

3. **Tests** (`packages/civic/tests/test_integrity.py`, 32 tests):
   - Hash consistency: same input always produces same hash
   - Hash sensitivity: tiny changes produce different hashes
   - Edge cases: empty/None inputs return None gracefully
   - Verification: matching and mismatched hashes
   - Dict key ordering: consistent hashing regardless of insertion order

### Files Changed
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/integrity.py (new)
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py (modified)
- packages/civic/tests/test_integrity.py (new)
- pilot.json (3 items -> ready)

- Pilot: 209/230 items ready (90%)
- 3 items completed this session

## Session 415: Vector Indexing Full-Text Audit

### Investigation: Full-Length Document Indexing

Audited vector indexing across all corpus types to verify full-length documents are being indexed correctly.

### Issues Found

| Corpus Type | Issue | Severity |
|-------------|-------|----------|
| **municipal_code** | BUG: `_municipal_code_to_text()` looks for `content` but table has `full_text` | 🔴 Critical |
| **municipal_code** | Truncation to 4000 chars even if field bug were fixed | 🟡 Medium |
| **legislation** | No `full_text` column in PostgreSQL - only summary stored | 🟡 Design gap |
| **legislation** | Dual indexing paths (pgvector + ChromaDB via LegalIndexer) | 🟡 Architecture |

### Architecture Decision

Both municipal_code and legislation should follow the **transcript pattern**:
- Store full text in PostgreSQL
- Use chunker (LegalChunker) via dependency injection
- Index chunks to pgvector

This is the right approach for long documents because:
1. `LegalChunker` already exists with section-aware splitting (1000 char chunks, 100 char overlap)
2. Preserves legal structure (sections, chapters, articles)
3. Same pattern as `TranscriptChunker` - proven to work
4. Single source of truth (PostgreSQL) instead of dual ChromaDB path

### Items Added to pilot.json

5 new items in `data_architecture.embedding_infrastructure`:

1. **municipal_code_indexing_bug** (P1): Fix `content` → `full_text` field name
2. **municipal_code_full_text_chunking** (P1): Replace 4000-char truncation with LegalChunker
3. **legislation_full_text_storage** (P1): Add `full_text` column to legislation table
4. **legislation_full_text_chunking** (P2): Use LegalChunker for legislation (depends on above)
5. **deprecate_chromadb_legal_indexer** (P3): Remove redundant LegalIndexer → ChromaDB path

### Engineering Patterns Documented

Each item includes a `pattern` field documenting the engineering best practice:
- "Field name must match PostgreSQL schema"
- "Dependency injection for chunkers - storage layer should not import from _internal"
- "Single source of truth - all corpus data in PostgreSQL, vectors in pgvector"
- "Consistent chunking across legal corpus types"

### Corpus Status Summary

| Corpus | Storage | Chunking | Vector Index | Status |
|--------|---------|----------|--------------|--------|
| chunks | full text | pre-chunked | ✅ pgvector | Working |
| decisions | metadata | N/A | ✅ pgvector | Working |
| meetings | metadata | N/A | ✅ pgvector | Working |
| transcripts | full JSONB | TranscriptChunker | ✅ pgvector | Working (Session 413) |
| issues | metadata | N/A | ✅ pgvector | Working |
| municipal_code | full_text | None (truncated) | ❌ Bug | Needs fix |
| legislation | summary only | None | ⚠️ Partial | Needs full_text column |

### Key Files Referenced

- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/pgvector_backend.py:455-460` - municipal_code bug
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/pgvector_backend.py:488-520` - legislation summary-only
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py:530-550` - municipal_code schema (has full_text)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py:569-592` - legislation schema (no full_text)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/legal/embeddings/chunker.py` - LegalChunker (ready to use)

- Pilot: 218/239 items (91% - added 5 new items)

## Session 424 (2026-01-01)

### Goal
Speed up vector indexing to <10 minutes for 5857 municipal_code chunks

### What Was Done
1. Added GPU support (T4) to Modal - NOT the bottleneck
2. Replaced individual INSERTs with bulk `execute_values()` 
3. Added deduplication to fix ON CONFLICT errors
4. Pre-cached embedding model in Docker image
5. Added parallel worker infrastructure (needs more work)

### Root Cause Found
**Database writes are the bottleneck**, not GPU:
- batch_size=100 = 59 DB transactions
- Each transaction = ~10s network latency to Neon
- Rate: ~140 vectors/min (need ~600/min for <10 min)

### Options for Next Session
- A: Add --batch-size CLI flag, use 1000
- B: Use COPY instead of INSERT (10x faster)
- C: Fix parallel workers to pre-expand then distribute

### Files Changed
- `scripts/modal_vectors.py` - GPU, parallel workers, delete_vectors
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/pgvector_backend.py` - bulk inserts
- `pilot.json` - Updated P0 note

### Current State
- Municipal code vectors: ~2400/5857 (partial)
- P0 remains: full_reindex_with_chunking

- `0f21202` Session 424: Add GPU + bulk insert optimizations
- `508a319` Session 424: Prepare handoff for next session

## Session 425 (2026-01-01)

### Goal
Continue P0: full_reindex_with_chunking - achieve <10 min indexing

### Key Bug Fixed: Chunk ID Uniqueness
**Problem**: Municipal code had duplicate section_numbers across chapters (e.g., `1.04.010` exists in 8+ chapters). With 3811 raw sections but only 2364 unique section_numbers, the chunker generated 5857 chunks but only 3344 unique IDs. ON CONFLICT caused most chunks to be skipped.

**Solution**: Use database `id` field (UUID) instead of section_number for chunk IDs.
- Chunk ID format: `mc-{db_id}-{chunk_index}` 
- Metadata includes `db_id`, `section_number`, `chapter` for traceback
- All 5857 chunks now have unique IDs

### Changes Made
1. **chunker.py**: Use `section.get("id")` for unique source_id
2. **modal_vectors.py**: batch_size 100→1000, fix _cost iteration bug
3. **Removed civic_extraction from Modal image** (not needed for vector indexing)

### Results
- **Before**: 3344/5857 municipal_code vectors (57% due to ID collisions)
- **After**: 5857/5857 municipal_code vectors (100%)
- **Time**: ~20 min (still above 10 min target)
- **Remaining bottleneck**: DB write latency to Neon

### Data Quality Issue Discovered
municipal_code table has 12,364 rows but only 2,364 unique sections. Multiple ingestion runs created duplicates. This should be cleaned up but is separate from vector indexing.

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/legal/embeddings/chunker.py` - db_id for unique IDs
- `scripts/modal_vectors.py` - batch_size, _cost fix, remove civic_extraction
- `pilot.json` - Update P0 note

### P0 Remains
full_reindex_with_chunking - need COPY command or parallel workers to hit <10 min target

## Session 426 - 2026-01-01

### Task: full_reindex_with_chunking (P0 → READY)

**Goal:** Reduce municipal_code indexing time from ~20 min to <10 min

### Changes

1. **pgvector_backend.py**: Added `use_copy` parameter to `index_from_storage`
   - When `use_copy=True`, uses PostgreSQL COPY instead of execute_values
   - COPY is ~10x faster for bulk inserts
   - Only safe when existing vectors deleted first (reindex mode)

2. **modal_vectors.py**: 
   - Pass `use_copy=reindex` to both single-worker and parallel modes
   - Fixed parallel distribution bug: was using section count (3811) instead of chunk count (5857)
   - Added `chunk_count` field to stats for chunked corpora

### Results

| Mode | Time | Improvement |
|------|------|-------------|
| Baseline (Session 425) | ~20 min | - |
| Single-worker + COPY | ~14 min | 30% faster |
| 4 parallel workers + COPY | **3:16** | **6x faster** |

Target was <10 min. Achieved **3 min 16 sec** - exceeded by 3x.

### Files Changed
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/pgvector_backend.py` - COPY bulk insert
- `scripts/modal_vectors.py` - use_copy param, chunk_count stats, fixed distribution

## Session 430: uscode_appendices - Complete U.S. Code Appendix Ingestion

**Date:** 2026-01-02
**Item:** uscode_appendices (pilot_validation > e2e_cloud_data_verification)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Fixed USCodeParser to handle appendix XML structure and ingested U.S. Code appendices to PostgreSQL.

### Problem
Appendices (5a, 11a, 18a, 28a) failed with `NotNullViolation: null value in column "title_number"`. The parser expected `<main>/<title>` structure but appendices use `<appendix>` and `<docNumber>` metadata.

### Solution
Updated `USCodeParser._load()` in `scripts/modal_uscode.py`:
1. Try standard `<main>/<title>` parsing first
2. Fallback to `<docNumber>` from metadata for appendices (e.g., "18a")
3. Extract numeric title_number from doc number (18a → 18)
4. Generate proper citations: "18 U.S.C. App. § 1" for appendices

### Results
| Appendix | Sections | Reason |
|----------|----------|--------|
| 5a | 0 | All transferred/repealed (Federal Advisory Committee Act) |
| 11a | 0 | All repealed (Bankruptcy appendix) |
| 18a | **26** | Criminal procedure rules, detainers act, classified info |
| 28a | 0 | Contains `<courtRule>` elements, not `<section>` |

### Database Status
- **Total sections:** 50,809 (up from 50,783)
- **New:** 26 sections from Title 18 Appendix
- **Release point:** PL 119-59 (Jan 2026)

### Files Changed
- `scripts/modal_uscode.py` - Parser fix for appendix XML, added appendices to ALL_TITLES
- `pilot.json` - uscode_appendices marked ready

### Verification
```python
# Query confirms appendix sections are searchable
what_applies("detainers")
# Returns: 18 U.S.C. App. § 2: Enactment into law of Interstate Agreement on Detainers
```

## Session 431: ca_codes_ingestion - Complete California Codes Ingestion

**Date:** 2026-01-02
**Item:** ca_codes_ingestion (pilot_validation > e2e_cloud_data_verification)
**Priority:** P0

### Summary
Ingested all 29 California Codes (161,219 sections) to PostgreSQL via Modal. Combined with existing U.S. Code data (50,809 sections), Civic now has **212,028 searchable codified law sections**.

### Implementation

1. **Data Source Analysis:**
   - Source: https://downloads.leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/pubinfo_2025.zip (650MB)
   - Format: Tab-separated metadata (LAW_SECTION_TBL.dat) + XML content files (.lob)
   - 29 codes: BPC, CIV, CCP, COM, CORP, EDC, ELEC, EVID, FAM, FIN, FGC, FAC, GOV, HNC, HSC, INS, LAB, MVC, PEN, PROB, PCC, PRC, PUC, RTC, SHC, UIC, VEH, WAT, WIC

2. **Scripts Created:**
   - `scripts/modal_cacode.py` - Modal function for cloud ingestion
   - `scripts/upload_cacode_r2.py` - Upload local ZIP to R2

3. **Parser Features:**
   - Handles California's CAML XML format: `<caml:Content xmlns:caml="..."><p>text</p></caml:Content>`
   - Proper citation format: "Cal. Gov. Code § 12345" with standard abbreviations
   - Hierarchical metadata: division, title, part, chapter, article
   - Effective date and history tracking

### Database Status

|-------------|----------|--------|
| state-CA | 161,219 | California Codes (29 codes) |
| federal-US | 50,809 | U.S. Code (57 titles including appendices) |
| **Total** | **212,028** | Combined codified law |

## Session 435 (2026-01-02)
**Item**: budget_etl_template
**Status**: COMPLETE

### Summary

Created AI-assisted budget extraction prompt template module for open-source contribution. The module enables any LLM (Claude, GPT-4, Gemini) to extract structured budget data from municipal PDF documents.

### Implementation

Created `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/prompts/` directory with:

1. **budget_extraction.py** - Core module with:
   - `BudgetExtractionPrompt` - Renders complete extraction prompt
   - `BudgetExtractionResult` - Parses and validates LLM responses
   - `BudgetLineItem` / `BudgetTotals` - Data classes matching StorageBackend schema
   - Layer 2 accuracy rules embedded in prompt (from BUDGET_ACCURACY_STRATEGY.md):
     - Prevents citywide budget confusion
     - Excludes investment portfolio values
     - Prevents context pollution
     - Detects double-counting
   - Validation with expected totals comparison
   - Dollar/cents conversion utilities

2. **test_budget_extraction.py** - 25 tests covering:
   - Dollar/cents conversions
   - JSON serialization/parsing
   - Markdown code block stripping
   - Validation logic (variance detection, negative amounts, suspiciously large values)
   - Prompt content verification

### Key Design Decisions

- **Model-agnostic**: Prompt works with any LLM capable of JSON output
- **Cents storage**: All amounts stored as integers (cents) to avoid floating-point errors
- **Validation built-in**: Result class has validate() method for accuracy checking
- **Layer 2 rules**: Critical budget extraction rules embedded in prompt to prevent common errors

### Test Results

```
pytest packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_budget_extraction.py -v → 25 passed
pytest packages/civic/tests/test_civic.py -q → 34 passed (smoke tests)
```

- packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_budget_extraction.py (new, 200 lines)

## Session 439: federal_awards_schema - Add Federal Awards Table Schema

**Date:** 2026-01-02
**Item:** federal_awards_schema (data_readiness > intergovernmental_funding)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Added federal_awards schema to StorageBackend protocol and PostgresBackend implementation. This is the first item in a 7-item sequence for intergovernmental funding tracking (federal→state→city funding flows). Data will come from USAspending.gov API.

### Implementation

1. **StorageBackend protocol** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py`):
   - `store_federal_awards(jurisdiction_id, awards, as_of) -> int`
   - `get_federal_awards(jurisdiction_id, cfda_number, period_start, period_end, as_of, limit) -> List[Dict]`
   - `get_federal_awards_count(jurisdiction_id) -> int`

2. **PostgresBackend** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py`):
   - Created `federal_awards` table with temporal versioning
   - Schema fields: award_id, cfda_number, recipient_uei, recipient_name, amount_cents, period_start, period_end, program_name, awarding_agency, funding_agency, award_type, metadata
   - Amounts stored in BIGINT cents for precision
   - Indexes on (jurisdiction_id, cfda_number), (recipient_uei), (period_start, period_end)
   - Uses execute_values for bulk inserts with temporal versioning

3. **SQLiteBackend stubs** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py`):
   - Added stub implementations returning empty/0 (Postgres used in production)

4. **Test updates** (`packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py`):
   - Added federal_awards methods to MockStorageBackend and InMemoryStorage
   - Added federal_awards to PostgresBackend required_methods check

### Files Changed

## Session 443 - 2026-01-02

### Completed: ca_grants_ingestion

Created California Grants Portal client for ingesting state grant data from grants.ca.gov.

**Key Implementation Details:**
- Uses data.ca.gov CKAN API (resource ID: 111c8c88-21f6-453c-ae2c-b4785a0624f5)
- Data updates daily at 8:45pm PT
- ~1,800 grant opportunities in portal (July 2020 onward)
- No API key required - free public API

**CaliforniaGrantsClient features:**
- health() and validate() for monitoring
- get_grants() - fetch all grants with filtering
- get_grants_for_local_government() - filter for Public Agency applicants
- get_housing_grants() - filter for Housing category
- get_grants_by_agency() - filter by CA agency name
- Amount parsing handles "$30,000,000", "1.5 million", ranges
- Fiscal year calculation (CA FY runs July 1 - June 30)

**Schema mapping to state_passthrough_funds:**
- passthrough_id: "ca-{PortalID}"
- state_agency: AgencyDept field
- state_program_name: Title field
- local_amount_cents: EstAvailFunds (converted to cents)
- federal_amount_cents: If FundingSource contains "Federal"
- period_start/period_end: OpenDate/ApplicationDeadline
- state_fiscal_year: Derived from period_start

**Note:** grants.ca.gov tracks grant OPPORTUNITIES, not specific awards to cities.
The amounts represent total available funding, not city-specific allocations.
Useful for tracking what funding is available to pursue.

P0 set to `budget_funding_source_linking` - Link budget line items to their federal/state funding sources.

## Session 444 - 2026-01-02

### Completed: budget_funding_source_linking

Implemented budget-funding source linking infrastructure to connect city budget line items to their federal/state funding sources. This enables "trace this dollar to source" queries.

**Key Implementation Details:**

1. **Storage Layer** (`budget_funding_source_links` table):
   - Links budget_item_id to federal_award_id and/or passthrough_id
   - Stores CFDA numbers for cross-referencing
   - Match confidence scoring (0.0 to 1.0)
   - Match type: cfda_exact, cfda_passthrough, cfda_federal, cfda_only, program_name, state_grant_id, ai_suggested, manual
   - Reconciliation status: match, variance, unverified
   - Human confirmation workflow (confirmed_by, confirmed_at)
   - Temporal versioning like all other tables

2. **StorageBackend Protocol** (4 new methods):
   - `store_budget_funding_links(jurisdiction_id, links, as_of) -> int`
   - `get_budget_funding_links(jurisdiction_id, budget_item_id, federal_cfda_number, match_type, confirmed_only, as_of, limit) -> List[Dict]`
   - `get_budget_funding_links_count(jurisdiction_id, confirmed_only) -> int`
   - `confirm_budget_funding_link(jurisdiction_id, link_id, confirmed_by) -> bool`

3. **Matching Engine** (`packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/funding/matcher.py`):
   - FundingMatcher class for automated link generation
   - extract_cfda_numbers() regex pattern for CFDA format (XX.XXX)
   - PROGRAM_CFDA_MAP with 25+ common federal programs (HUD, DOT, EPA, FEMA, DOJ, HHS)
   - Multi-stage matching: 
     - Stage 1: Direct CFDA extraction from text (conf: 0.95)
     - Stage 2: Program name keyword matching (conf: 0.80)
     - Stage 3: State grant ID matching (conf: 0.85)
   - Match object with to_link() for storage conversion
   - Automatic reconciliation status calculation (match <1%, variance <10%, else unverified)

4. **Test Coverage**:
   - Added budget_funding_links methods to MockStorageBackend and InMemoryStorage test classes
   - 78 storage protocol tests pass
   - 39 smoke tests pass

### Schema Design

```sql
CREATE TABLE budget_funding_source_links (
    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    link_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    jurisdiction_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    budget_item_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    federal_award_id TEXT,
    federal_cfda_number TEXT,
    passthrough_id TEXT,
    state_grant_id TEXT,
    match_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    match_confidence DECIMAL(3,2) NOT NULL,
    match_source TEXT,
    match_notes TEXT,
    budget_cents BIGINT,
    federal_cents BIGINT,
    local_cents BIGINT,
    reconciliation_status TEXT,
    variance_cents BIGINT,
    variance_percentage DECIMAL(5,2),
    confirmed_by TEXT,
    confirmed_at TIMESTAMP,
    valid_from TIMESTAMP,
    valid_to TIMESTAMP,
    ...
)
```

P0 set to `funding_reconciliation` - Reconcile federal award amounts with city budget receipts.

## Session 445 - 2026-01-02

### Completed: funding_reconciliation

Implemented aggregate funding reconciliation to validate budget-award alignment. The reconciler compares SUM(city budget grants) with SUM(federal/state awards to city) and flags discrepancies >5%.

**Key Implementation Details:**

1. **FundingReconciler Class** (`packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/funding/reconciler.py`):
   - Loads budget items and funding links
   - Calculates per-link variance (budget_cents vs award_cents)
   - Flags items with variance >5% for review
   - Generates comprehensive ReconciliationReport

2. **ReconciliationReport** dataclass:
   - Summary stats: total/linked/unlinked budget items
   - Amount totals: budget_cents, linked_budget_cents, award_cents
   - Status counts: matched (<1%), variance (<10%), unverified, flagged (>5%)
   - Properties: link_rate, overall_variance_pct, is_reconciled
   - to_dict() for JSON serialization
   - flagged_items list for review

3. **ReconciliationItem** dataclass:
   - Individual link reconciliation result
   - needs_review property for >5% variance
   - Status: match, variance, unverified, flagged

4. **Edge Case Handling**:
   - Indirect cost variance (10-15%) noted in comments
   - State pass-through timing lag noted
   - Multi-year award indicators detected
   - local_cents preferred over federal_cents (more specific)

5. **Test Coverage**:
   - 20 new tests in test_funding_reconciler.py
   - Tests for ReconciliationItem, ReconciliationReport, FundingReconciler
   - Edge case tests for indirect costs, pass-through timing

### Files Changed

- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/funding/reconciler.py` (new)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/funding/__init__.py` (exports added)
- `packages/civic/tests/test_funding_reconciler.py` (new)

## Session 446 (2026-01-02)

### Completed: funding_flow_api

Implemented `Civic.funding_flow()` method for tracing intergovernmental dollars from federal → state → city budget items.

**Intergovernmental Funding Sequence** (7 of 8 complete):
1. ~~federal_awards_schema~~ - Session 439
2. ~~usaspending_ingestion~~ - Session 440
3. ~~state_passthrough_schema~~ - Session 442
4. ~~ca_grants_ingestion~~ - Session 443
5. ~~budget_funding_source_linking~~ - Session 444
6. ~~funding_reconciliation~~ - Session 445
7. ~~funding_flow_api~~ - Session 446 (THIS SESSION)
8. populate_federal_state_awards - P0 (NEXT)

### Implementation Details

1. **FundingFlow dataclass** (civic.py:331-371):
   - Complete funding chain: federal → state → city
   - Budget item info (id, description, dollars, department, fund, fiscal_year)
   - Federal info (award_id, cfda, program_name, agency, dollars, period)
   - State passthrough info (passthrough_id, agency, grant_id, dollars, period)
   - Match quality (type, confidence, reconciliation_status, variance)

2. **FundingFlowImpact dataclass** (civic.py:374-385):
   - For "what if" analysis (e.g., "what if CDBG cut 20%?")
   - program_name, cfda_number, cut_percentage
   - total_current_dollars, total_impact_dollars
   - affected_items list

3. **Civic.funding_flow() method** (civic.py:1101-1244):
   - Filters: program, cfda_number, budget_item_id, fiscal_year, min_confidence
   - Queries storage for budget items, funding links, federal awards, passthroughs
   - Builds lookup maps and constructs FundingFlow objects
   - Returns List[FundingFlow]

4. **Civic.funding_flow_impact() method** (civic.py:1246-1304):
   - Calls funding_flow() then calculates cut impact
   - Returns FundingFlowImpact with affected items and totals

5. **Tests** (test_funding_flow.py):
   - 21 tests covering dataclasses, methods, and mocked data scenarios
   - All passing

## Session 448 (2026-01-02)

### Task: funding_flow_e2e → fac_ingestion_client

Started investigating why `funding_flow()` showed "Unknown" for budget descriptions.

### Findings

1. **Jurisdiction ID mismatch** - Storage layer didn't normalize jurisdiction IDs. Fixed by adding `normalize_jurisdiction()` to all funding-related methods in postgres_backend.py.

2. **Keyword matching is unreliable** - The FundingMatcher linked "Measure C Wildfire Prevention" ($3.9M local ballot measure) to a federal firefighters grant ($190K) because both contain "fire". This is a spurious match.

3. **No explicit linkage in budget data** - Budget PDFs don't contain CFDA numbers or grant IDs. Amount-based matching produces false positives (23 matches, almost all coincidental).

4. **The solution: Federal Audit Clearinghouse** - Cities must file Single Audits with audited Schedule of Expenditures of Federal Awards (SEFA). This has explicit CFDA→expenditure linkages.

### Changes

- Added `normalize_jurisdiction()` to 8 postgres_backend methods
- Migrated data from `san-rafael` to `city-san-rafael`
- Capped `variance_percentage` to 999.99 in FundingMatcher
- Removed spurious keyword-based funding links
- Added `fac_ingestion_client` P0 item to pilot.json

### Next Session

P0: `fac_ingestion_client` - Build client to ingest SEFA data from Federal Audit Clearinghouse (app.fac.gov). This provides audited federal grant data with explicit CFDA linkages.

## Session 449 (2026-01-02)

### Task: fac_ingestion_client (P0)

Built Federal Audit Clearinghouse (FAC) client to ingest audited federal expenditure data.

### What We Built

1. **FederalAuditClearinghouseClient** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/fac.py`)
   - Connects to api.fac.gov (requires API key from api.data.gov)
   - Search audits by entity name, city, state
   - Retrieve federal award expenditures (SEFA data)
   - Normalize to civic storage format
   - Rate limiting and exponential backoff

2. **PostgreSQL Storage** (`packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py`)
   - New `federal_audit_expenditures` table with temporal versioning
   - Fields: report_id, cfda_number, audit_year, amount_expended_cents, program_name, etc.
   - Methods: `store_federal_audit_expenditures()`, `get_federal_audit_expenditures()`

3. **Civic API** (`packages/civic/src/civic/civic.py`)
   - `federal_expenditures(cfda_number, audit_year)` - Get audited expenditure records
   - `federal_expenditures_summary(audit_year)` - Summary by program
   - New `FederalExpenditure` dataclass

### Data Ingested

San Rafael Single Audits (2016-2023): 7 audits, 52 expenditure records

FY2023 Federal Expenditures ($2.0M total):
- 93.778 Medicaid: $658K
- 20.205 Highway Planning: $637K  
- 97.036 FEMA Disaster Grants: $562K [MAJOR]
- 20.600 Highway Safety: $86K
- 97.039 Hazard Mitigation: $55K
- 14.218 CDBG: $23K

FY2022 spike to $19M due to SLFRF COVID relief funds.

### Key Insight

FAC data is audited expenditures (what the city actually spent) vs USAspending which shows award amounts (what was allocated). FAC is the authoritative source for "how much did we spend from federal grant X?"

## Session 451: funding_flow_e2e - Document Funding Data Sources and Limitations

**Date:** 2026-01-03
**Item:** funding_flow_e2e (data_readiness > intergovernmental_funding)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Documented the distinction between FAC (audited expenditures) and USAspending (direct awards), clarified API capabilities and limitations, and identified CA State Controller as a high-value data source for state/county revenue.

### Key Findings

1. **FAC vs USAspending**: Most local government federal funding is pass-through (state agencies administer federal grants). FAC captures this; USAspending misses it.

2. **CA State Controller** (bythenumbers.sco.ca.gov): Structured Socrata API with:
   - FY2024 data available (more recent than FAC FY2023)
   - State revenue: $3.5M/year for San Rafael
   - County revenue: $900K/year
   - 20+ years of history

3. **Budget→Grant Linkage Gap**: Can't automatically link budget line items to federal grants because city budgets don't contain CFDA numbers.

### Files Changed

- `packages/civic/src/civic/civic.py` - Updated docstrings for funding_flow() and federal_expenditures()
- `docs/critical/FEDERAL_FUNDING_DATA_SOURCES.md` - New comprehensive documentation
- `pilot.json` - Marked funding_flow_e2e ready, added ca_state_controller_ingestion task

## Session 453 (continued): extraction_config_financial_section

**Date:** 2026-01-03
**Item:** extraction_config_financial_section (data_architecture > financial_data_infrastructure)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Added `FinancialConfig` dataclass to `ExtractionConfig` to enable config-driven financial client creation. This centralizes financial data source parameters (SCO, FAC, USAspending) in the extraction config JSON files.

### Implementation

1. **FinancialConfig dataclass** (`packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/base.py`):
   - `state`: State code (e.g., "CA")
   - `county`: County name (e.g., "Marin")
   - `sco_entity_name`: Entity name for CA State Controller
   - `fac_auditee_name`, `fac_auditee_city`: FAC search parameters
   - `uei`: Unique Entity Identifier for USAspending
   - `usaspending_recipient_name`: Recipient name for USAspending
   - `budget_pdf_pattern`, `acfr_pdf_pattern`: URL templates
   - `fiscal_year_start_month`: Fiscal year start (default 7 = July)

## Session 455 (2026-01-03)

### Completed: budget_acfr_reconciliation_automation (P0)

Extended `scripts/reconcile_funding_sources.py` to output structured JSON reconciliation reports with IngestionManifest integration for provenance tracking.

### Changes

1. **FundingMapping.to_dict()**: Added JSON serialization method to the dataclass

2. **create_reconciliation_report()**: New function that generates structured JSON report with:
   - Jurisdiction and fiscal year metadata
   - Summary statistics (matched/unmatched counts, by_source_type breakdown)
   - Full list of reconciliation items

3. **save_reconciliation_with_manifest()**: New function that:
   - Runs reconciliation
   - Creates `IngestionManifest` for provenance tracking
   - Adds file checksums for budget and ACFR inputs
   - Saves both report JSON and manifest JSON
   - Cross-references manifest ID in report

4. **CLI enhancements**:
   - `--save`: Enable JSON output (default: human-readable only)
   - `--output`: Custom output directory (default: `data/reconciliation/`)
   - `--no-manifest`: Skip manifest for debugging
   - `--quiet`: Suppress human-readable output

### Output Structure

**Report JSON** (`data/reconciliation/reconciliation-{manifest_id}.json`):
```json
{
  "jurisdiction_id": "city-san-rafael",
  "budget_fiscal_year": "2025-2026",
  "acfr_fiscal_year": "2024-2025",
  "summary": { "total_items": 58, "matched_items": 20, ... },
  "items": [{ "budget_item": "...", "source_type": "state", ... }],
  "manifest_id": "ingest_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_city-san-rafael"
}
```

**Manifest JSON** (`data/reconciliation/manifests/{jurisdiction}/ingest_*.json`):
- Input file checksums (budget, acfr, report)
- Reconciliation algorithm version
- Source entry with records_ingested/skipped counts

## Session 464 (2026-01-03)
**Item**: `elected_officials_table` (P0)

### Summary
Created storage mapper functions to link elected officials from RepresentativesClient to the elected_officials table in storage backends.

### Changes Made
1. **Added mapper functions to `representatives.py`** (lines 815-981):
   - `_generate_name_variations(name, office)` - Generates name variations for roll call matching (e.g., "Jane Smith" → ["Jane Smith", "Smith", "J. Smith", "Councilmember Smith"])
   - `representative_to_elected_official(rep, jurisdiction_id)` - Maps Representative dataclass to storage dict format
   - `extract_elected_officials_to_storage(client, storage, jurisdiction_id, ...)` - End-to-end extraction helper
   - `ElectedOfficialStorageProtocol` - Protocol for storage type checking

2. **Updated exports in `clients/__init__.py`**:
   - Added `representative_to_elected_official`, `extract_elected_officials_to_storage`, `ElectedOfficialStorageProtocol`

3. **Added 14 tests in `test_clients.py`**:
   - `TestElectedOfficialsMappers` (9 unit tests): name variations, mapper with full/partial data
   - `TestElectedOfficialsExtraction` (3 unit tests): empty list, with data, level filtering
   - `TestElectedOfficialsStorageIntegration` (2 integration tests): SQLite storage round-trip, name lookup

### Key Files
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/representatives.py` (lines 815-981)
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/__init__.py` (exports)
- `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_clients.py` (TestElectedOfficialsMappers, TestElectedOfficialsExtraction, TestElectedOfficialsStorageIntegration)

### Usage Example
```python
from civic_extraction.clients import (
    create_san_rafael_representatives_client,
    extract_elected_officials_to_storage,
)
from civic.storage import SQLiteBackend

client = create_san_rafael_representatives_client()
storage = SQLiteBackend("civic_state.db")

# Store all officials (local only - no API keys needed)
count = extract_elected_officials_to_storage(
    client=client,
    storage=storage,
    jurisdiction_id="san-rafael",
    include_local=True,
    include_federal=False,  # Requires CONGRESS_GOV_API_KEY
    include_state=False,    # Requires OPENSTATES_API_KEY
)
print(f"Stored {count} officials")

# Retrieve
officials = storage.get_elected_officials("san-rafael", current_only=True)
```

## Session 465: roll_call_extraction - Extract Vote Patterns from Minutes

**Date:** 2026-01-03
**Item:** roll_call_extraction (data_readiness > election_data)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Implemented roll call vote extraction from city council meeting minutes. The system now parses AYES/NOES/ABSENT vote patterns to populate the `vote_results` field in decisions.

### Implementation

1. **Roll call extraction functions** (`packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/meetings/decision.py`):
   - `extract_roll_call(text)` - Parses AYES/NOES/ABSENT patterns, returns {"ayes": [], "noes": [], "absent": []}
   - `extract_motion_attribution(text)` - Extracts "X moved, Y seconded" patterns
   - `extract_vote_tally(text)` - Combines roll call + motion into VoteTally object
   - `normalize_vote_names(tally, officials)` - Matches extracted names to elected officials via name_variations

2. **Helper functions**:
   - `_parse_names(text)` - Handles comma/ampersand separation, "None" values
   - `_strip_title(name)` - Removes "Mayor", "Vice Mayor", "Councilmember" prefixes
   - `_find_official(name, lookup, officials, match_func)` - Fuzzy matching

3. **VoteTally enhancement**:
   - Added `to_vote_results()` method for converting to {"Name": "yes/no/absent"} format

4. **Patterns handled** (from real San Rafael minutes):
   - `AYES: Councilmembers: Bushey, Hill, Kertz & Mayor Kate`
   - `NOES: Councilmembers: None`
   - `ABSENT: Councilmembers: Llorens Gulati`
   - `Vice Mayor Bushey moved, and Councilmember Kertz seconded`

### Tests

Created `packages/civic/tests/test_roll_call_extraction.py` with 31 tests:
- TestExtractRollCall (7 tests) - AYES/NOES/ABSENT pattern variations
- TestParseNames (6 tests) - Name list parsing
- TestStripTitle (5 tests) - Title prefix removal
- TestExtractMotionAttribution (4 tests) - Motion/second patterns
- TestExtractVoteTally (3 tests) - Complete vote extraction
- TestNormalizeVoteNames (3 tests) - Name normalization
- TestRealMinutesPatterns (3 tests) - Actual Oct 6 minutes patterns

## Session 469 - 2026-01-04

### P0: decision_extraction_diagnosis

**Objective**: Diagnose full pipeline completeness issue (duplicates, chunks, decisions)

### Findings

1. **Meeting "Duplicates" - MISDIAGNOSIS CORRECTED**
   - 97 rows from temporal versioning, NOT duplicates
   - 46 unique meeting IDs with version history
   - Only 1 "current" meeting (valid_to IS NULL)
   - Temporal versioning design: `store_meetings()` closes ALL meetings, inserts only current scrape window
   - **Not a bug** - but historical meetings require valid_to queries

2. **Chunk Extraction Gap - ROOT CAUSE**
   - 5,084 chunks exist (extracted Dec 28-29)
   - Most recent meeting_ids in chunks: October 2025
   - `civic-extract chunks --cloud` is NOT in `modal_ingest.py`
   - **Fix**: Add to `scheduled_high_velocity_refresh`

3. **Decision Extraction Gap - ROOT CAUSE**
   - 44 decisions (Oct 1 - Dec 15, 2025)
   - `batch_extract_decisions.py` is a manual script
   - **Fix**: Add to weekly schedule (minutes PDFs lag meetings)

### Changes

## Session 471 (2026-01-05)

### Focus: temporal_versioning_review (P0)

**Problem**: `store_meetings()` was closing ALL meeting records when called,
leaving only meetings in the current scrape window visible. This broke:
- `get_meetings()` - returned only 1 of 46 meetings
- `extract_chunks()` - couldn't see historical meetings
- `extract_decisions()` - same issue

**Root Cause**: Original implementation pattern:
```python
# Close ALL previous versions
UPDATE meetings SET valid_to = NOW() WHERE jurisdiction_id = X
# Insert only current scrape window
INSERT new meetings
```

**Fix**: Changed to proper upsert pattern:
- Check if meeting exists by ID
- Compare key fields to detect changes
- Only close old version if data actually changed
- Preserve meetings not in current scrape window

**Data Restoration**: Ran SQL to reopen 45 meetings:
```sql
WITH latest_versions AS (...)
UPDATE meetings SET valid_to = NULL WHERE ...
```

**Changes**:
- `postgres_backend.py`: Fixed `store_meetings()` upsert logic
- `sqlite_backend.py`: Same fix for consistency
- `backend.py`: Updated protocol docstring to clarify semantics
- Cloud data: 45 meetings reopened (46 total now visible)
- pilot.json: `temporal_versioning_review` → ready

**Pipeline Critic Warning**: Addressed by updating docstrings to clarify:
- Return value is count of meetings stored/updated (not count passed in)
- Meetings lacking 'id' field are skipped
- Meetings not in input list are preserved

### Verification

```bash
# Before fix
get_meetings() → 1 meeting

# After fix
get_meetings() → 46 meetings
```

### Next Session: automated_decision_extraction (P0)

Now that meetings are visible, the next step is to add decision extraction
to the automated Modal pipeline.

## Session 472 (2026-01-05)

### Completed: automated_agenda_extraction (P0)

**Problem**: `whats_next()` returned meetings but no agenda items. Only 44 generic metadata entries ("Agenda", "Agenda Packet") existed in the database - not actual agenda items like "7.1 Housing Element Update".

**Root Cause**: The CLI's `extract_agenda_items_from_meeting()` was calling `parse_agenda_content(agenda_url)` with the meeting PAGE URL (HTML), not the actual PDF URL. The LLM correctly extracted what it saw - the download links on the page.

**Fix** (packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/agenda.py):
1. Changed `extract_agenda_items_from_meeting()` to call `AgendaIntegrator.discover_agenda_url()` first
2. This discovers the actual PDF URL from the meeting page
3. Then `parse_agenda_content()` parses the PDF content correctly

**Added to scripts/modal_ingest.py**:
1. `extract_agenda_items()` function - LLM-powered agenda extraction
2. Added to `scheduled_low_velocity_refresh` (weekly schedule)
3. Added `--agenda` flag to main entrypoint

**Results**:
- Backfill in progress: ~80+ items from ~20+ meetings (vs 44 metadata entries before)
- ~59 actionable items extracted
- City Council meetings now have proper numbered items (7.a, 5.b, etc.)

**Note**: `whats_next()` uses StateManager (SQLite), not PostgresBackend. Agenda items are now in Postgres but `whats_next()` won't see them until the Civic API is updated to use `_storage` for meetings+agenda items.

### Files Changed:
- packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/agenda.py (PDF discovery fix)
- scripts/modal_ingest.py (Modal function + schedule + entrypoint)

## Session 473: storage_protocol_agenda_items - Protocol Conformance Fix

**Date:** 2026-01-05
**Item:** storage_protocol_agenda_items (data_integrity > architectural_debt)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Fixed protocol conformance violation flagged by Session 472's protocol critic. The `store_agenda_items()`, `get_agenda_items()`, and `get_agenda_item_count()` methods existed in PostgresBackend but were NOT defined in the StorageBackend protocol, violating the protocol conformance pattern.

### Implementation

1. **Added Agenda Item Methods section to StorageBackend protocol** (`backend.py` lines 461-527):
   - `store_agenda_items(meeting_id, agenda_items, as_of)` → int
   - `get_agenda_items(meeting_id, jurisdiction_id, as_of, limit)` → List[Dict]
   - `get_agenda_item_count(jurisdiction_id)` → int
   - Full docstrings with Args/Returns
   - Comment block explaining purpose (whats_next() queries)

2. **Added stub methods to SQLiteBackend** (`sqlite_backend.py` lines 1608-1653):
   - Following pattern of other Postgres-primary features (Issues, Municipal Code)
   - Returns 0/[] for stubs
   - Matching signatures with protocol

3. **Verified conformance programmatically:**
   - All parameter names match between protocol and implementations
   - SQLiteBackend passes `isinstance(sqlite, StorageBackend)` check

### Files Changed:
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/backend.py (protocol definition)
- packages/civic/src/civic/storage/sqlite_backend.py (stub implementation)
- pilot.json (marked ready, set next P0)

### automated_decision_extraction (P0) - COMPLETE

Added decision extraction to Modal automated pipeline (`scripts/modal_ingest.py`).

**Changes:**
1. Added `extract_decisions()` function (lines 900-986) - LLM-powered extraction using `run_decision_extraction()` from `civic_extraction.cli.decisions`
2. Added CLI args: `--decisions`, `--decisions-limit`
3. Added to weekly schedule (`scheduled_low_velocity_refresh`) - runs Sunday 3 AM UTC
4. Decisions NOT in `--all` flag (weekly only, not daily) because meeting minutes PDFs lag behind meetings

**Pattern followed:** Same as `extract_agenda_items()` - uses existing CLI function with `cloud=True`

**Why weekly, not daily:**
- Meeting minutes PDFs are typically published 1-2 weeks after meetings
- Running daily would waste LLM costs on meetings without available minutes
- Placed in `scheduled_low_velocity_refresh` alongside municipal code and legislation

**Verification:** `python3 -m py_compile scripts/modal_ingest.py` passes

**Usage:**
```bash
modal run scripts/modal_ingest.py --decisions                    # All meetings
modal run scripts/modal_ingest.py --decisions --decisions-limit 5  # Limit to 5
modal run scripts/modal_ingest.py --decisions --dry-run           # Preview
```

## Session 476 (2026-01-05)

**Completed:** `vector_sql_sync_verification` (P0)

**Changes:**
1. Added `--verify-sync` and `--sync-threshold` options to `civic-extract vectors` CLI
2. Added `verify_sync()` function in `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/vectors.py`
3. Added "Verify vector-SQL sync" step to `.github/workflows/vector-refresh.yml` after indexing
4. Updated `pilot.json` to mark item complete

**Implementation Details:**
- Verification compares pgvector counts to PostgreSQL row counts
- SQL counts use `valid_to IS NULL` filter (temporal versioning - only count current versions)
- 15% threshold used in workflow to accommodate issues without text content being skipped
- Checks: decisions, meetings, issues (skips chunks/transcripts/municipal_code which expand during indexing)

**Issues Mismatch Explained:**
- Session 469 found 1430 vectors vs 1630 SQL issues (12% mismatch)
- Root cause: Issues with empty type/summary/description/address/status produce no text
- These are skipped during `_issue_to_text()` -> `index_from_storage()` (line 695-696)
- This is expected behavior, not data corruption

## Session 483: pdf_hash_storage - PDF Provenance Tracking

**Date:** 2026-01-06
**Item:** pdf_hash_storage (data_integrity > source_provenance)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Implemented PDF hash storage for chunk provenance, mirroring Session 482's audio_hash pattern. This enables verification that chunks came from specific, unmodified PDF source files.

### Implementation

1. **Added `compute_pdf_hash()` and `verify_pdf_hash()` to `integrity.py:166-200`:**
   - SHA-256 hash of raw PDF bytes
   - Same pattern as compute_audio_hash()
   - Returns None for empty/None input

2. **Added schema migrations in `postgres_backend.py:1199-1259`:**
   - Added content_hash columns to transcripts, chunks, decisions (SESSION 479)
   - Added audio_hash to transcripts (SESSION 482)
   - Added pdf_hash to chunks (SESSION 483)
   - All migrations use IF NOT EXISTS pattern

3. **Updated `store_chunks()` in `postgres_backend.py:2355-2381`:**
   - Extracts pdf_hash from chunk dict
   - Inserts into pdf_hash column

4. **Updated `extract_chunks_from_meeting()` in `chunks.py:851-866`:**
   - Computes pdf_hash from pdf_bytes before chunk creation
   - All chunks from same PDF share the same hash
   - Graceful ImportError fallback

5. **Added 12 tests in `test_integrity.py:368-442`:**
   - TestComputePdfHash (5 tests): consistency, sensitivity, large file, edge cases
   - TestVerifyPdfHash (7 tests): matching, mismatched, case-insensitive, edge cases

### Data Flow

```
PDF URL → download_and_validate_pdf() → pdf_bytes
        → compute_pdf_hash(pdf_bytes) → pdf_hash
        → AgendaPacketParser.parse() → agenda_chunks
        → chunks_data[i]["pdf_hash"] = pdf_hash
        → store_chunks_to_cloud()
        → PostgresBackend.store_chunks() [hash stored]
```

**Tests:** 56/56 integrity tests pass, 39/39 smoke tests pass

**Next P0:** extraction_versioning (track which extractor version produced each record)

## Session 484: extraction_versioning - Track Extractor Versions

**Date:** 2026-01-06
**Item:** extraction_versioning (data_integrity > source_provenance)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary
Implemented extraction version tracking to record which version of the civic-extraction package produced each record. This enables selective re-extraction when extractors improve.

### Implementation

1. **Updated Meeting dataclass** (`base.py:310-328`):
   - Added `extraction_version: Optional[str] = None` field
   - Updated `to_dict()` to include extraction_version in serialization

2. **Updated Pipeline._run_ingest()** (`pipeline.py:547-558`):
   - Imports `_get_version()` from manifest.py
   - Injects extraction_version into each meeting after fetching
   - Records extraction_version in stage metadata

3. **Updated PostgresBackend store methods** to populate extraction_version column:
   - `store_meetings()` (line 1391): Added extraction_version to INSERT
   - `store_chunks()` (line 2366): Added extraction_version to INSERT
   - `store_decisions()` (line 1971): Added extraction_version to INSERT
   - `store_transcripts()` (line 2749): Added extraction_version to INSERT
   - `store_issues()` (line 3234): Added extraction_version to INSERT
   - `store_municipal_code()` (line 2975): Added extraction_version to INSERT

4. **Added 5 tests** (`test_postgres_backend.py:384-519`):
   - TestExtractionVersioning class
   - Tests for meetings, chunks, decisions, issues with/without extraction_version
   - Tests skip when PostgreSQL not available (run in CI)

### Schema
Columns already existed from migration 015_data_integrity.sql:
- transcripts.extraction_version
- chunks.extraction_version
- decisions.extraction_version
- meetings.extraction_version
- issues.extraction_version
- municipal_code.extraction_version

### Data Flow
```
civic-extraction:_get_version() → "0.1.0"
Pipeline._run_ingest() → meetings[i].extraction_version = version
Pipeline._run_store() → storage_target.store_meetings(meetings)
PostgresBackend.store_meetings() → INSERT ... extraction_version = %s
```

**Tests:** 39/39 smoke tests pass, 5 extraction versioning tests added (skip locally, run in CI)

## Session 494: ab_test_retrieval_quality - Validate pgvector Retrieval Quality

**Date:** 2026-01-08
**Item:** ab_test_retrieval_quality (data_architecture > embedding_infrastructure)
**Priority:** P0 (IMMEDIATE)

### Summary

Created comprehensive retrieval quality validation framework for pgvector. Since pilot data is stored exclusively in pgvector (not duplicated in ChromaDB), the tests validate quality through score validation, term relevance, and cross-corpus search behavior.

### Test Results

All 10 tests pass. Key quality metrics:

**Corpus Quality (25 queries across 5 corpus types):**
- municipal_code: Avg top score 0.758, term relevance 69.7%
- chunks: Avg top score 0.660, term relevance 75.4%
- transcripts: Avg top score 0.684, term relevance 57.9%
- issues: Avg top score 0.722, term relevance 41.7%
- decisions: Avg top score 0.559, term relevance 13.3%

**Quality Gates:**
- [PASS] All 25 queries return results
- [PASS] Average top score 0.677 > 0.6
- [PASS] Average term relevance 51.6% > 10%

### Key Observations

1. **ChromaDB Empty:** Local ChromaDB has no data. All pilot data is in pgvector. Original A/B comparison approach was pivoted to quality validation.

2. **Score Ranges Valid:** All scores in (0, 1] cosine similarity range as expected.

3. **Cross-Corpus Search:** UnifiedSearch.search_all() returns results from multiple source types (pdf, municipal_code).

4. **Decisions Lower Quality:** Decision queries have lower term relevance (13.3%) - likely due to semantic search finding relevant content that doesn't contain exact expected terms. Scores still reasonable (0.467-0.659).

## Session: 2026-01-08 - incremental_vector_indexing (P0)

### Summary

Fixed critical vector indexing bug where `--reindex` would delete ALL vectors
corpus-wide but only re-index documents from filtered storage query, causing
permanent data loss.

### Changes Made

1. **Default behavior is now incremental-safe**
   - Uses ON CONFLICT upsert (never deletes existing vectors)
   - `use_copy=False` is now the default mode
   
2. **Added safety check to --reindex**
   - Checks if existing_vectors > to_index_count
   - Blocks with clear error message if would cause data loss
   - Must use `--force` to proceed with destructive operation

3. **Added --force flag**
   - Allows explicit override of safety check
   - Required for any `--reindex` where delete > create

4. **Comprehensive tests added**
   - `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_vectors_cli.py`
   - 8 unit tests covering all safety scenarios
   - All tests pass

### Files Changed

- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/vectors.py` - Safety logic
- `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_vectors_cli.py` - New test file
- `pilot.json` - incremental_vector_indexing → ready

### Next P0

eval_framework - Expand benchmark with F1, coverage, and bias metrics

## Session: 2026-01-09 - simbli_pdf_download (P0)

### Completed
Implemented PDF download workflow for Simbli school board agendas using captured MIDs.

### Implementation

1. **Updated `get_agenda_pdf()` method** (simbli.py:846-869)
   - Tries direct URL first, falls back to MID-based download
   - Enables PDF download even when no direct agenda URL is available

2. **Implemented `download_agenda_pdf_via_mid(mid)` method** (simbli.py:871-957)
   - Navigates to PrintAgenda.aspx?S={district}&MID={mid}
   - Clicks Print button and captures API response using `expect_response`
   - Parses FileUrl from JSON response: `{"IsPass": true, "FileUrl": "..."}`
   - Downloads PDF from FileUrl using `page.request.get()`

3. **Added `_download_pdf_from_url()` helper** (simbli.py:959-1000)
   - Handles relative/absolute URLs
   - Downloads PDF using Playwright's request context

### Tests Added

**Unit Tests** (test_clients.py:2608-2747 - TestSimbliPdfDownload class):
- test_get_agenda_pdf_with_direct_url_only
- test_get_agenda_pdf_falls_back_to_mid
- test_get_agenda_pdf_with_mid_only
- test_get_agenda_pdf_returns_none_when_no_url_or_mid
- test_download_agenda_pdf_via_mid_returns_none_for_empty_mid
- test_download_pdf_from_url_makes_absolute

**Integration Test** (test_clients.py:2816-2848):
- test_download_agenda_pdf_via_mid - downloads real 843KB PDF (7 pages)

### Test Results
- 31 Simbli unit tests pass (6 new)
- 5 Simbli integration tests pass (1 new)
- Downloaded PDF verified: 843,004 bytes, PDF v1.4, 7 pages

### Key Technical Details

The Simbli PDF download workflow involves:
1. Navigate to PrintAgenda.aspx establishes session context
2. Print button click triggers POST to app2.eboardsolutions.com/api/PrintAgenda/PrintAgenda
3. API returns JSON: `{"IsPass": true, "FileUrl": "https://app2.eboardsolutions.com/api/PrintAgenda/GetFile/..."}`
4. FileUrl contains time-limited token (fid parameter)
5. GET FileUrl returns the actual PDF bytes

Using `page.expect_response()` instead of event listeners proved more reliable for capturing the API response.

### Files Changed
- packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/clients/simbli.py
- packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_clients.py
- pilot.json (status: ready, completed: 2026-01-09)

## Session 492: srcs_r2_pdf_storage - Direct R2 Upload for School Board PDFs

**Date:** 2026-01-09
**Item:** srcs_r2_pdf_storage (data_readiness > school_district)
**Priority:** P0

### Summary

Modified `scripts/ingest_srcs.py` to upload SRCS agenda PDFs directly to R2 during the download process, rather than as a separate upload step. This aligns with the user's request to have scrapers/ingestion directly download to R2.

### Implementation

1. **Direct R2 Upload Integration:**
   - PDFs are uploaded to R2 immediately after download via SimbliClient
   - Key pattern: `school-san-rafael/agendas/{meeting_id}.pdf`
   - Uses `get_blob_storage()` factory to auto-detect R2 vs local storage
   - Gracefully degrades if R2 is unavailable (continues with local processing)

2. **Agenda URL Updates:**
   - Updates `meetings.agenda_url` with R2 URL after upload
   - Added `_update_meeting_agenda_url()` helper function
   - Enables Modal workers to access PDFs for LLM extraction

3. **New CLI Flag:**
   - `--skip-r2` flag to skip R2 upload (local processing only)
   - Default behavior now uploads to R2

### Files Changed
- `scripts/ingest_srcs.py` - Direct R2 upload during PDF download
- `pilot.json` - Updated srcs_r2_pdf_storage status to ready

### Usage

```bash
# Full ingestion with R2 upload (default)
python scripts/ingest_srcs.py

# Skip R2 upload (local processing only)
python scripts/ingest_srcs.py --skip-r2

# Dry run (shows R2 key paths)
python scripts/ingest_srcs.py --dry-run
```

### Verification

```bash
# Check R2 uploads
python3 -c "
from dotenv import load_dotenv; load_dotenv()
from civic.storage.blob import get_blob_storage
blob = get_blob_storage()
keys = blob.list_keys('school-san-rafael/agendas/')
print(f'PDFs in R2: {len(keys)}')
for k in keys: print(f'  {k}')
"

# Check agenda_urls in DB
python3 -c "
from dotenv import load_dotenv; load_dotenv()
import os, psycopg2
conn = psycopg2.connect(os.getenv('DATABASE_URL'))
cur = conn.cursor()
cur.execute('''
    SELECT COUNT(*) FROM meetings 
    WHERE jurisdiction_id = 'school-san-rafael' 
    AND agenda_url LIKE 'r2://%'
''')
print(f'Meetings with R2 agenda_url: {cur.fetchone()[0]}')
"
```

## Session 493: srcs_agenda_item_extraction - SRCS LLM Agenda Extraction

**Date:** 2026-01-09
**Item:** srcs_agenda_item_extraction (data_readiness > school_district)
**Priority:** P0

### Summary

Extracted structured agenda items from SRCS PDFs using Gemini LLM. Required three fixes:
1. Added R2 URL handling to `parse_agenda_content()` 
2. Backfilled R2 agenda URLs for 43 meetings missing them
3. Increased `max_tokens` from 2000 to 6000 for large agendas

### Implementation

1. **R2 URL Support in AgendaIntegrator:**
   - Modified `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/processing/agenda_integration.py`
   - Added R2 blob download before HTTP fallback
   - PDF detection for R2 URLs (assume PDF since we store PDFs)

2. **R2 Backfill Script:**
   - Added `--backfill-r2` mode to `scripts/ingest_srcs.py`
   - Downloads PDFs via Simbli MID workflow
   - Uploads to R2, updates `meetings.agenda_url`
   - Backfilled 43 meetings (40 successful)

3. **Fixed Token Truncation Issue:**
   - Root cause: `max_tokens=2000` too low for agendas with 10+ items
   - LLM responses were truncated mid-JSON, causing parse failures
   - Increased to 6000 (Gemini 1.5 Pro max: 8192)
   - SRCS extraction went from 0 items to 598 items

### Results

- **598 agenda items** extracted from 33 meetings
- Items have project types: `governance`, `education`
- Actionability classification: `actionable` vs `informational`

## Session 496: Transcript Duration Validation (2026-01-09)

### P0 Item: transcript_duration_validation

**Problem:** Session 480 identified transcripts with durations exceeding actual YouTube video length (e.g., 169 min stored vs 47 min actual for iYeihDimgxE). This causes timestamp URLs to fail when t= exceeds video length. Root cause: audio download grabbing playlist or concatenated streams instead of single video.

**Solution implemented:**
1. Added `validate_transcript_duration()` function comparing AssemblyAI duration against YouTube API duration
2. Added `get_youtube_video_duration()` to fetch video duration from YouTube Data API
3. New database columns: `youtube_duration_seconds`, `duration_valid`, `duration_validation_error` 
4. Validation integrated into `transcribe_audio_file()` - runs automatically during transcription
5. New CLI command: `civic-extract validate-transcripts --jurisdiction <id> [--update]`

**Key files modified:**
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/transcribe.py` - Added validation functions and CLI command
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/postgres_backend.py` - Added migration for new columns, updated store_transcripts()
- `packages/civic-extraction/src/civic_extraction/cli/__init__.py` - Registered new CLI command

**Validation results for city-san-rafael:**
- Valid: 10 transcripts
- Invalid: 9 transcripts (corrupted audio from playlist concatenation)
- Tolerance: 10% to allow for encoding overhead

**Tests added:**
- `packages/civic-extraction/tests/test_transcribe.py` - 11 tests for duration validation
- Unit tests for validation logic, tolerance boundaries
- Integration tests with known mismatched transcripts (QLDoO6OvMSA, iYeihDimgxE)

- Decisions: 5% sync tolerance

## Session 493 (Parallel): hallucination_detection - Hallucination Detection for Eval Framework

**Date:** 2026-01-10
**Item:** hallucination_detection (test_infrastructure > test_categorization)
**Priority:** P2
**Track:** INFRA (parallel session, different from P0 DATA track)
**Branch:** feature/infra/eval-framework

### Summary

Added hallucination detection to the eval framework. This feature analyzes LLM baseline responses to demonstrate the value of RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation) by showing how often baseline LLMs make unverifiable claims compared to our grounded Civic API responses.

### Implementation

1. **HallucinationDetector class** in `scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py`:
   - Extracts factual claims from LLM responses using LLM
   - Classifies claims as verifiable (specific facts) or unverifiable (generic advice)
   - Verifies verifiable claims against the Civic database
   - Calculates hallucination metrics

2. **CLI Integration:**
   - `--hallucination-check` flag enables hallucination detection
   - Integrates with existing `--clear-cache` flag
   - Results displayed in new "HALLUCINATION ANALYSIS" section

3. **Metrics Added:**
   - `verified_claims` - Claims supported by database evidence
   - `unverified_claims` - Claims that can't be verified (potential hallucinations)
   - `unverifiable_claims` - Generic advice (not hallucinations)
   - `grounding_rate` - verified / verifiable (higher = better)
   - `hallucination_risk` - unverified / verifiable (lower = better)

### Usage

```bash
# With hallucination detection
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py --hallucination-check

# Full analysis (both LLM judge and hallucination detection)
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py --llm-judge --hallucination-check

# Clear all caches
python scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py --clear-cache
```

### Files Changed
- `scripts/benchmark_api_vs_llm.py` - Added HallucinationDetector class, Claim dataclass, HallucinationReport dataclass
- `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` - Added hallucination detection documentation
- `pilot.json` - Updated hallucination_detection status to ready

### Commits
- 0b25211 feat: Add hallucination detection to eval framework

### Status
- [x] Implementation complete
- [x] Documentation added
- [x] pilot.json updated
- [x] Pushed to origin
- [x] Merged (Session 501)

## Session 505: fix_ci_false_positives - CI Reliability Fix

**Date:** 2026-01-10
**Item:** fix_ci_false_positives (test_infrastructure > ci_reliability)
**Priority:** P0

### Summary

Fixed 2 categories of CI test failures that were false positives, not actual bugs.

### Work Completed

1. **Fixed security test false positive** - `test_database_connection_strings_not_logged`
   - Issue: Regex `logger\.\w+\([^)]*database_url` was matching benign messages like `logger.error("DATABASE_URL not set")`
   - Fix: Added `safe_patterns` filter to exclude messages that mention the env var name but don't log actual values
   - Safe patterns: "not set", "not configured", "environment variable not set", "Required for", "Available (", "Set DATABASE_URL"

2. **Fixed Protocol runtime_checkable tests** - `TestVectorBackendProtocol`, `TestStorageBackendProtocol`, `TestProtocolIntegration`
   - Issue: Mock classes used `@dataclass` decorator, but Python's `isinstance()` on `@runtime_checkable` Protocol requires all method names to exist
   - Fix: Converted `@dataclass` mocks to regular classes with `__init__` and `@property` decorators
   - Added 14 missing methods to mocks: agenda items (3), elections (9), update_meeting, VectorBackend.count()

### Tests Fixed

- `test_database_connection_strings_not_logged`: 25 violations → 0 violations
- `TestVectorBackendProtocol`: 2 failures → 4 passes
- `TestStorageBackendProtocol`: 1 failure → 1 pass
- `TestProtocolIntegration`: 1 failure → 1 pass

### Files Modified

- `packages/civic/tests/test_e2e_verification.py` (+15 lines)
- `packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py` (+260 lines)
- `pilot.json` (status: ready, next P0: federal_programs_postgres_migration)

### Session 506 - 2026-01-10: Storage Backend Protocol Decomposition

**Status:** COMPLETE ✓

**P0 Completed:** storage_backend_decomposition

Decomposed the 82-method StorageBackend protocol into 6 domain-specific sub-protocols:

1. **ContentStorage** (20 methods): meetings, decisions, chunks, agenda_items, transcripts, videos
2. **LegislationStorage** (17 methods): legislation, municipal_code, codified_law, executive_orders
3. **FinancialStorage** (20 methods): budget_items, federal_awards, state_passthrough, budget_funding_links, federal_programs
4. **CommunityStorage** (3 methods): issues (311 complaints)
5. **ElectionStorage** (11 methods): elections, deadlines, contests, officials
6. **OperationsStorage** (8 methods): operations, etl_costs

### Files Created

- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/protocols/__init__.py`
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/protocols/content.py`
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/protocols/legislation.py`
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/protocols/financial.py`
- `packages/civic/src/civic/storage/protocols/community.py`
