- `packages/civic/tests/test_storage_protocols.py` - Added 5 new tests for sub-protocols

### Tests

- 89/89 protocol tests passing (84 original + 5 new)
- 42/42 smoke tests passing
- Backward compatible - StorageBackend still works as before

### Architecture Benefits

- Logical grouping of methods by domain
- Sub-protocols can be used for narrower type constraints
- Easier testing of domain-specific functionality
- Future growth: new domains become new protocols
- MRO: StorageBackend → ContentStorage → LegislationStorage → FinancialStorage → CommunityStorage → ElectionStorage → OperationsStorage → Protocol

## Session 507: civic_api_decomposition Phase 1 - Mixin Infrastructure

**Date:** 2026-01-10
**Item:** civic_api_decomposition (data_architecture > architecture_audit)
**Priority:** P0 → P2 (completed phase 1)

### Summary

Created the infrastructure for decomposing the 10,456-line civic_api_integrated.py into domain-specific handlers using Python's multiple inheritance mixin pattern. This is Phase 1 of a two-phase approach, with FastAPI migration as Phase 2.

### Approach Decision

Evaluated two approaches:
1. **FastAPI migration** - Modern framework with automatic OpenAPI docs, Pydantic validation
2. **Mixin pattern** - Lower risk, incremental decomposition

Chose to implement mixin infrastructure first because:
- Pilot is Jan 2026 - stability matters more than elegance
- Current system works (42/42 tests passing)
- Decomposition unlocks FastAPI - once handlers are in separate files, migration is easier
- Added `fastapi_migration` as P0 for next session

### Work Completed

1. **Created routers/ directory structure:**
   - `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers/__init__.py`
   - `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers/base.py` - Router/Route classes
   - `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers/core.py` - CoreMixin

2. **CoreMixin handlers extracted:**
   - `serve_status()` - Health check with system checks
   - `serve_jurisdictions()` - List jurisdictions with metadata
   - `serve_google_maps_key()` - Frontend config endpoint

3. **Integration via multiple inheritance:**
   - Changed: `class AuthenticatedCivicAPIHandler(CoreMixin, BaseHTTPRequestHandler)`
   - MRO verified: AuthenticatedCivicAPIHandler → CoreMixin → BaseHTTPRequestHandler → ...

4. **Updated pilot.json:**
   - Marked `civic_api_decomposition` as ready (Phase 1 complete)
   - Added `fastapi_migration` as new P0

### Files Created

- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers/__init__.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers/base.py` (~250 lines)
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers/core.py` (~250 lines)

### Files Modified

- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/civic_api_integrated.py` - Added CoreMixin inheritance
- `pilot.json` - Updated status, added fastapi_migration item

### Tests

- 42/42 smoke tests passing
- Import verification successful
- MRO verified at runtime

### Next P0: fastapi_migration

Migrate API from BaseHTTPRequestHandler to FastAPI with proper routers. Benefits:
- Automatic OpenAPI documentation at /docs
- Pydantic request/response validation
- Native async/await support
- Cleaner route definitions with decorators
- Industry-standard Python API framework

## Session 508 - FastAPI Migration (2026-01-10)

### Task: fastapi_migration (P0)

Migrated the Civic API from BaseHTTPRequestHandler to FastAPI.

### Changes

1. **Added Dependencies**
   - `requirements.txt`: Added fastapi>=0.109.0, uvicorn[standard]>=0.27.0, python-multipart>=0.0.6

2. **Created FastAPI Application**
   - `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/civic_api_fastapi.py` - Main FastAPI app with:
     - CORS middleware configuration
     - Request logging middleware
     - Rate limiting checks
     - Health check endpoint

3. **Created Domain Routers** (`packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/`)
   - `core.py` - /api/status, /api/jurisdictions, /help, /api/onboarding/cards, /api/config/google-maps-key
   - `events.py` - /api/events, /api/events/search, /api/events/{id}, /api/events/{id}/discussion-stats
   - `issues.py` - /api/issues, /api/issues/search, /api/issues/{id}, /api/issues/{id}/timeline, etc.
   - `admin.py` - /api/admin/cache-stats, /api/admin/operations, /api/admin/vector-stats, etc.
   - `user.py` - /api/user/profile, /api/user/location, /api/user/export, DELETE /api/user
   - `follows.py` - /api/follows/{focal_type}/{focal_id}, POST /api/follows
   - `threads.py` - /api/threads, /api/threads/{id}/messages
   - `legislative.py` - /api/legislation/state/{topic}, /api/elections/{jurisdiction_id}
   - `conversations.py` - POST /api/conversation, POST /api/chat/route
   - `drafts.py` - /api/events/{id}/drafts, POST /api/drafts/{id}/submit
   - `dependencies.py` - Shared auth dependencies (verify_auth, get_api_keys)

4. **Updated scripts/dev.sh**
   - Added `api-fastapi` command to launch with uvicorn
   - Added `USE_FASTAPI=true` environment variable option
   - OpenAPI docs available at /docs, ReDoc at /redoc

### Architecture

The FastAPI implementation provides:
- **65 routes** across 10 domain routers
- **Automatic OpenAPI documentation** at /docs
- **Pydantic models** for request/response validation
- **Native async/await** support
- **Bearer token authentication** via FastAPI dependencies
- **Rate limiting** preserved from original implementation
- **CORS** via middleware

### Usage

```bash
# Launch FastAPI server directly
./scripts/dev.sh api-fastapi

# Or set USE_FASTAPI=true for the api command
USE_FASTAPI=true ./scripts/dev.sh api

# Access OpenAPI docs
open http://localhost:8001/docs
```

### Tests

- 42/42 smoke tests passing
- FastAPI app loads successfully with all routers

### Files Modified

- `requirements.txt`
- `scripts/dev.sh`
- `pilot.json`

### Files Created

- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/civic_api_fastapi.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/__init__.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/dependencies.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/core.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/events.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/issues.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/admin.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/user.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/follows.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/threads.py`
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/servers/routers_fastapi/legislative.py`

## Session 509: jurisdiction_config_system - Architecture Audit

**Date:** 2026-01-11
**Item:** jurisdiction_config_system (data_architecture > architecture_audit)
**Priority:** P0 (completed)

### Summary

Extended the existing JurisdictionConfig system to fully replace hardcoded San Rafael references throughout the codebase. The infrastructure already existed in `civic-config` - this task was about completing adoption.

### Work Completed

1. **Extended JurisdictionConfig dataclass** (`packages/civic-config/src/civic_config/jurisdiction.py`):
   - Added `display_name: str` - Human-readable name (e.g., "San Rafael")
   - Added `hall_name: str` - Meeting location (e.g., "San Rafael City Hall")
   - Added `domains: Tuple[str, ...]` - Associated domains for URL detection
   - Added `wiki_files: Tuple[str, ...]` - Wiki documentation paths

2. **Added JurisdictionRegistry methods**:
   - `get_by_domain(domain)` - Lookup config by domain name
   - `get_hall_name(jurisdiction_id)` - Get meeting hall name
   - `get_display_name(jurisdiction_id)` - Get human-readable name
   - `get_wiki_files(jurisdiction_id)` - Get wiki file paths
   - `get_jurisdiction_id_by_domain(domain)` - Get jurisdiction_id from domain
   - `get_location_display_name(location_mention)` - Resolve city name to jurisdiction_id

3. **Updated jurisdiction configs** with extended fields:
   - San Rafael (pilot city) - full config
   - Berkeley - full config
   - Santa Rosa, Hayward, Oakland - display_name, hall_name, domains
   - El Cerrito - display_name, hall_name, domains
   - **Added Marin County** (wasn't in registry, now added)

4. **Fixed hardcoded references**:
   - `minutes.py:_extract_location()` - Now uses JurisdictionRegistry.get_hall_name()
   - `civic_digest.py:_detect_jurisdiction()` - Now uses domain-based lookup
   - `civic_digest.py:_load_wiki_files()` - Now uses JurisdictionRegistry.get_wiki_files()
   - `issue_detector.py:_resolve_jurisdiction()` - Now uses get_location_display_name()

### Test Results
- 42/42 smoke tests passing
- New JurisdictionRegistry methods verified working

### Files Modified
- `packages/civic-config/src/civic_config/jurisdiction.py` (extended config + new methods)
- `packages/civic/src/civic/_internal/meetings/minutes.py` (fixed hardcoded location)
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/processing/civic_digest.py` (fixed maps)
- `packages/civic-services/src/civic_services/issues/issue_detector.py` (fixed location_map)
- `pilot.json` (marked ready)

## Session 512 - 2026-01-11

### Task: pilot_json_consolidation (P0)

Consolidated pilot.json from 364 individual items to a more compact structure:
- **Before**: 183KB, 364 items tracked individually
- **After**: 57KB (69% reduction), completed items grouped in `_completed_items` arrays
- Ready items: 324 (89%)
- Not ready items: 38 (still tracked individually for active work)

### Changes Made
1. **pilot.json restructured**:
   - Completed items grouped by subcategory into `_completed_items` arrays
   - Not-ready items preserved individually with full metadata
   - Preserves all counting and priority detection

2. **init.sh updated**:
   - Added list handling to `count_items()` function
   - Now correctly counts items in `_completed_items` arrays

### Technical Approach
- Each subcategory now has:
  - `_completed_items`: array of `{name, status: 'ready'}` for completed work
  - Individual items for `not_ready` work (preserves priority, description, etc.)
- `/start` command works unchanged (skips non-dict items)
- `init.sh` counts both formats correctly

### Files Changed
- `pilot.json` - Restructured (183KB → 57KB)
- `init.sh` - Updated count_items() to handle lists
- `pilot_original.json` - Backup of original (can be deleted)

### Test Results
- 42/42 smoke tests passing
- `init.sh` reports: 324/362 items ready (89%)
- `/start` correctly identifies P0 item
